J(\epsilon)=\int_{0}^{\epsilon}\sqrt{1+\log N(\bm{X},\|\cdot\|,t)}\mathrm{d}t
. By the maximal inequality in Corollary 2.2.8 in
van der Vaart and Wellner (1996)
,we have

\mathbb{E}[|\overline{\Delta W_{\varepsilon}^{\max}}||{X_{1:n}}]=\mathbb{E}\sup_{(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in\bm{X}_{\epsilon}^{2}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[\xi_{i}f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})-\xi_{i}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},X_{i})]\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}J(\eta\epsilon).

Plugging (
A.18
) into the above, we have

\mathbb{E}[|\Delta W_{\varepsilon}^{\max}||{X_{1:n}}]\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}J(\eta\epsilon)+K_{\mathcal{F}}\epsilon.

(A.19)

We then bound
\mathbb{E}[|\Delta T_{\varepsilon}^{\max}|]
. By Assumption
4.12
, we first have that for any
(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{2}),(\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{x}_{2}^{\prime})\in\bm{X}_{\epsilon}^{2}
,

\displaystyle|Z_{\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\prime}}-Z_{\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{2}^{\prime}}|\leq

\displaystyle|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[f_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{1},X_{i})-f_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{2},X_{i})]|+|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[f_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\prime},X_{i})-f_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{2}^{\prime},X_{i})]|

(A.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{F}}(||\mathbf{x}_{1}-\mathbf{x}_{2}||+||\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\prime}-\mathbf{x}_{2}^{\prime}||)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{F}}\cdot d_{\|\cdot\|}((\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\prime}),(\mathbf{x}_{2},\mathbf{x}_{2}^{\prime})),

where the distance
d_{\|\cdot\|}(\cdot)
on
\bm{X}_{\epsilon}^{2}
is defined as
d_{\|\cdot\|}((\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))=\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\|+\|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\|
.

Meanwhile, by Lemma
D.12
, we have

\log N(\bm{X}_{\epsilon}^{2},d_{\|\cdot\|},\delta)\leq\log N(\bm{X},\|\cdot\|,\delta/2)+\log N(B_{\|\cdot\|}(\mathbf{x},\epsilon+\delta),\|\cdot\|,\delta/2),

and by Assumption
4.13
and letting
\delta+\epsilon<1
, it follows that

\log N(\bm{X}_{\epsilon}^{2},d_{\|\cdot\|},\delta)\leq 2\log N(\bm{X},\|\cdot\|,\delta/4).

(A.21)

Let
\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}=\{Z_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}})|(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in\bm{X}_{\epsilon}^{2}\}
. By Assumption
4.11
, there exists an envelope function
F_{Z}(x)
such that
||F_{Z}||_{\infty}\leq 2\eta
. Following (
A.20
), by the standard Rademacher complexity technique and Assumption
4.12
, there exists a constant
K_{1}>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{T}[\sup_{\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon}}[Z_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}}-\mathbb{E}(Z_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{x}^{\prime}})]]\leq K_{1}\int_{0}^{2\eta}\sqrt{\frac{\log N(\mathcal{L}_{\epsilon},\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}},\delta)}{n}}\mathrm{d}\delta

\displaystyle\leq K_{1}\int_{0}^{2\eta\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{\log N(\bm{X}_{\epsilon}^{2},\|\cdot\|,K_{\mathcal{F}}\delta)}{n}}\mathrm{d}\delta

\displaystyle\leq K_{1}\int_{0}^{2\eta\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{2\log N(\bm{X},\|\cdot\|,K_{\mathcal{F}}\delta/4)}{n}}\mathrm{d}\delta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}K_{1}J(K_{\mathcal{F}}\eta\epsilon/2),

where the third equality follows (
A.21
).
Therefore, together with (
A.17
), we have

\mathbb{E}[|\Delta T_{\varepsilon}^{\max}|]\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}K_{1}J(K_{\mathcal{F}}\eta\epsilon/2)+K_{\mathcal{F}}\epsilon.

(A.22)

Combining (
A.19
) and (
A.22
), we have

\displaystyle\phi(\epsilon)\leq\max\Big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}J(\eta\epsilon)+K_{\mathcal{F}}\epsilon,K_{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}J(K_{\mathcal{F}}\eta\epsilon/2)+K_{\mathcal{F}}\epsilon\Big)\leq K_{1}\Big(\frac{J(\eta\epsilon)+J(K_{\mathcal{F}}\eta\epsilon/2)}{\sqrt{n}}+K_{\mathcal{F}}\epsilon\Big),

which completes the proof for bounding
\phi(\epsilon)
.

Now we apply the bound of
\phi(\epsilon)
to (
A.15
) and (
A.16
). Taking
\epsilon=1/n
, we have

\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{0}-\widetilde{T}_{0}\geq K_{1}J(\eta/n)+\frac{K_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)\leq 1-e^{-\xi},

and

\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathbb{P}\Big(W_{0}-\widetilde{W}_{0}\geq K_{1}J(\eta/n)+\frac{K_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big|X_{1:n}\Big)\geq 1-e^{-\xi}\Big)\leq 1-e^{-\xi},

for some positive
\xi
.

Applying the triangle inequality to (
A.10
) and (
A.11
), we have

\mathbb{P}\Big(|T-\widetilde{T}_{0}|>\zeta_{1}+K_{1}J(\eta/n)+\frac{K_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big)\leq\zeta_{2}(1-e^{-\xi}),

and

\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathbb{P}\Big(|W-\widetilde{W}_{0}|>\zeta_{1}+K_{1}J(\eta/n)+\frac{K_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\Big|X_{1:n}\Big)\geq\zeta_{2}(1-e^{-\xi})\Big)\leq\zeta_{2}(1-e^{-\xi}).

Therefore, there exist
\zeta_{1}^{\prime}=\zeta_{1}+K_{1}J(\eta/n)+\frac{K_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\text{ and }\zeta_{2}^{\prime}=\zeta_{2}(1-e^{-\xi})
such that

\mathbb{P}(|T-\widetilde{T}_{0}|>\zeta_{1}^{\prime})<\zeta_{2}^{\prime}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{P}(|W-\widetilde{W}_{0}|>\zeta_{1}^{\prime}|X_{1:n})>\zeta_{2}^{\prime})<\zeta_{2}^{\prime}.

Thus, we have shown that (
A.7
) is a sufficient condition of (
A.8
), which completes the proof.
∎

A.3
Proof of Theorem
4.7

Proof.

We first derive the non-asymptotic expansion of
T
. Let
\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})
and
\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})
be

\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{npL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big),

(A.23)

and

\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})=\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{npL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\psi^{\prime}(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})),

where
\psi(x)=\frac{e^{x}}{1+e^{x}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{im}=1
when model
i
and model
m
are selected and
\mathcal{E}_{im}=0
otherwise.

We apply the following lemma on the non-asymptotic expansion of
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(t)
, It is the rephrased version of Theorem 2.2 in
Gao et al. (2023)
. We provide it here for self-completeness.

Lemma A.3
.

[Non-Asymptotic Expansion for
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})
]
Given Assumptions
4.1
,
4.5
,
4.6
,
4.8
and
4.10
,
for any
m\in[n]
,

\widehat{\theta}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})=-(1+\epsilon_{1,m}(\mathbf{x}))\frac{\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}+\epsilon_{2,m}(\mathbf{x}),

(A.24)

holds with

\|\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\|_{\infty}=o(1),\ \ \ \|\bm{\epsilon}_{2}\|_{\infty}=o\bigg(dh^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg),

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. Specifically, define the infinite norm of the error term as

\|\bm{\epsilon}_{1}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\bigg|\epsilon_{1,m}(\mathbf{x})\bigg|,\ \ \ \|\bm{\epsilon}_{2}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\bigg|\epsilon_{2,m}(\mathbf{x})\bigg|.

By Lemma
A.3
, we have the non-asymptotic expansion of
T
such that

T_{0}=\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\bigg|\frac{\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|,

(A.25)

where
\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})
is defined as

\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})=\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})]=\frac{1}{npL}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\psi^{\prime}(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big].

(A.26)

To facilitate the proof, we let

T^{m}_{0}(\mathbf{x})=\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\bigg|\frac{\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|=\max\bigg(\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\frac{\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})},-\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\frac{\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg).

(A.27)

We also define the statistic
W_{0}
as the approximation of
T_{0}
by

W_{0}=\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\bigg|\frac{\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|,

(A.28)

where
\mathcal{G}{m}(\mathbf{x})
is defined as

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{npL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\xi_{im}^{\ell}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big).

Then, following Corollary
4.6
, we have confidence band for
\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})
. To ensure the validity of Corollary
4.6
, it remains to check the following conditions required in the corollary:

1.

There exist
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}
such that
\mathbb{P}(|T-T_{0}|>\zeta_{1})<\zeta_{2},\quad\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{P}(|W-W_{0}|>\zeta_{1}|y)>\zeta_{2})<\zeta_{2}\text{ with }\zeta_{1}\sqrt{\log(npL)}+\zeta_{2}\leq C_{0}(npL)^{-c_{0}}
, for some positive
c_{0},C_{0}
.

2.

We have
c\leq\mathbb{E}(T^{m}_{0}(\mathbf{x})^{2})\leq C
for some positive
c,C
.

For the first condition, by Lemmas
C.1
and
C.2
, we have that there exist
\zeta_{1}=o\Big(\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}\Big)
and
\zeta_{2}=O(n^{-10})
such that the required conditions hold.

Then, we consider the second condition. First, by Lemmas
D.1
and
D.2
, we have
\mathcal{V}_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})
is
O(1)
. For
\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})
, we have

\displaystyle\Var\big(\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)=\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\Var\bigg(\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \ =\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\sum_{i\neq m}\Var\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{im}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\ \ =\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\sum_{i\neq m}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Var\Big(\mathcal{E}_{im}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big)\big|\mathcal{E}_{im}\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\ \ \quad+\Var\bigg(\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{E}_{im}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big)\big|\mathcal{E}_{im}\Big]\bigg)\bigg),

where the second equality holds by the independence of
\mathcal{E}_{im}
and
\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}
with respect to
i
and the third holds by law of total variance.

Note that

\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{E}_{im}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big)\big|\mathcal{E}_{im}\Big]=0.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\Var\big(\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)=\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\sum_{i\neq m}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Var\Big(\mathcal{E}_{im}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big)\big|\mathcal{E}_{im}\Big)\bigg]\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt=\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\sum_{i\neq m}\bigg(\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{E}_{im}^{2}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\Var\Big(\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big)\Big)\bigg]\bigg).

We further apply Theorem 1 of
Härdle and Müller (1997)
such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\Var\Big(\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big)\Big)\leq\frac{C}{h^{d}}\|K\|_{2}^{2}\,

for some constant
C
. Therefore, we can conclude that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\Var\big(\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)=O\bigg(\frac{npL}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}h^{d}}\bigg)=O\bigg(\frac{1}{npLh^{d}}\bigg).

Since for any
(i,m)
, we have
\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big)]=0
,

T^{m}_{0}(\mathbf{x})
is unbiased. Therefore, we have
\mathbb{E}(T^{m}_{0}(\mathbf{x})^{2})=O(1)
, which completes the proof.
∎

A.4
Proof of Theorem
4.8

Proof.

We show that for the pairwise test, the Type I error can be well-controlled at the desired level, and the power is asymptotically one with some required signal strength. The proof for the top-
K
test is similar, and we skip it here to avoid repetition.

First, for the Type I error, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\exists\mathbf{x},\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\leq 0}\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{*}}(\text{reject }H_{0})\leq\sup_{\exists\mathbf{x},\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\leq 0}\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{*}}\Big(\inf_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}(\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\theta}_{j}(\mathbf{x}))>\widehat{c}_{ij}(1-\alpha)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sup_{\exists\mathbf{x},\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\leq 0}\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{*}}\bigg(\inf_{\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\leq 0}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\bigg[\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\theta}_{j}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})+\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\bigg]>\widehat{c}_{ij}(1-\alpha)\bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sup_{\exists\mathbf{x},\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\leq 0}\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{*}}\bigg(\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\bigg[\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\theta}_{j}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})+\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\bigg]>\widehat{c}_{ij}(1-\alpha)\bigg).

Let
\widetilde{T}_{ij}=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\bigg[\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\theta}_{j}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})+\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\bigg]
.
We show that there exists
\zeta_{1}=O(n^{-\epsilon/4}\sqrt{\log n})
and
\zeta_{2}=O(n^{-10})
such that the
(1-\alpha)\%
quantile of
W_{ij}
in (
3.12
) accurately estimates the
(1-\alpha)\%
quantile of
\widetilde{T}_{ij}
. The proof is similar to the proof of Lemma
C.1
and
C.2
. We skip it here to avoid repetition. Specifically, by Corollary
4.6
, we have that

\sup_{\alpha\in(0,1)}|\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{T}_{ij}\leq\widehat{c}_{ij}(1-\alpha))-(1-\alpha)|\xrightarrow{}0\text{ as }n\xrightarrow{}\infty.

Combining the two inequalities above, we have that

\sup_{\exists\mathbf{x},\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\leq 0}\mathbb{P}_{\theta^{*}}(\text{reject }H_{0})\leq\alpha+o(1),

which shows that the Type I error is controlled as desired.

Then, we show that the power goes to one asymptotically when the signal strength is strong enough. In particular, for a fixed
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\text{reject }H_{0})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\inf_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}(\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\theta}_{j}(\mathbf{x}))>\widehat{c}_{ij}(1-\alpha)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\inf_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\Big(\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\Big)-T_{ij}>\widehat{c}_{ij}(1-\alpha)\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\inf_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\Big(\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\Big)>2\delta,T_{ij}<\delta,\widehat{c}_{ij}(1-\alpha)<\delta\bigg).

By Theorem
4.3
, we have

\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{ij}\leq 2\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)\Big)\geq 1-O(n^{-10}).

(A.29)

Meanwhile, by Corollary
4.6
, we have for any fixed
\alpha
and sufficiently large
L,n
,

\mathbb{P}\Big(T_{ij}\geq\widehat{c}_{ij}(1-\alpha)\Big)\geq\alpha/2.

(A.30)

Combining (
A.29
) and (
A.30
), we have

\widehat{c}_{ij}(1-\alpha)\leq 2\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big),

for some sufficiently large constant
C_{0}
with probability
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Therefore, letting

\delta=C\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)

for some sufficiently large constant
C
and taking
h\leq({npLd^{2}})^{-\frac{1}{d+4}}
, we have

\inf_{\forall\mathbf{x},\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})>\delta}P(\text{reject }H_{0})>1-O(n^{-10}),

which completes the proof.
∎

A.5
Proof of Theorem
4.9

Proof.

Let
\widehat{\mathbf{H}}
be the output of Algorithm
2
after
\Gamma
iterations. For
\ell\in[\Gamma]
, we let the set of pairs that have been rejected by be algorithm be
\mathcal{S}_{\ell}
, and let the quantile be
\widehat{c}_{\ell}(\alpha)
. Since the confidence diagram is based on the pairwise test, we first have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(R(\bm{\theta}^{*})\in\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(\inf_{\ell\in[\Gamma]}\inf_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{S}_{\ell}^{C},T_{ij}>\widehat{c}_{\ell}(1-\alpha)}(\theta^{*}_{i}-\theta^{*}_{j})>0\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{\ell\in[\Gamma]}\sup_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{S}_{\ell}^{C},\theta^{*}_{i}-\theta^{*}_{j}\leq 0}(T_{ij}-\widehat{c}_{\ell}(1-\alpha))\leq 0\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\geq\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{\ell\in[\Gamma]}\sup_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{S}_{\ell}^{C},\theta^{*}_{i}-\theta^{*}_{j}\leq 0}\Big[\inf_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\Big(\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\theta}_{j}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})+\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\Big)-\widehat{c}_{\ell}(1-\alpha)\Big]\leq 0\Big)

Recall that in each iteration, we leave out all the pairs that are rejected in the previous iterations and update the quantile,
\widehat{c}_{\ell}(\alpha)
. Therefore, we have

\widehat{c}_{0}(\alpha)\geq\widehat{c}_{\ell}(\alpha)\geq\widehat{c}_{\Gamma}(\alpha).

(A.31)

Moreover, let
\widetilde{T}_{\ell}=\sup_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{S}_{\ell}^{C}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\Big(\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\theta}_{j}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})+\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\Big)
. By Corollary
4.6
, the
\alpha
-quantile of the corresponding statistic
W
derived via the Gaussian multiplier bootstrap accurately estimates the
\alpha
-quantile of
\widetilde{T}_{\ell}
that

\sup_{\alpha\in(0,1)}|\mathbb{P}(\widetilde{T}_{\ell}\leq\widehat{c}_{\ell}(\alpha))-\alpha|\xrightarrow{}0\text{ as }n\xrightarrow{}\infty.

(A.32)

Following (
A.31
) and (
A.32
),
Romano and Wolf (2005)
shows the strong control of the family-wise error rate such that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{\ell\in[\Gamma]}\sup_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{S}_{\ell}^{C}}(\inf_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\Big(\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\theta}_{j}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})+\theta^{*}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\Big)-\widehat{c}_{\ell}(1-\alpha))\leq 0\Big)\geq 1-\alpha+o(1).

(A.33)

Therefore, we have

\mathbb{P}\Big(R(\bm{\theta}^{*})\in\widehat{\mathbf{H}}\Big)\geq 1-\alpha+o(1),

which completes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Proof of Lemmas in Section
A.1

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

Consider any
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
and
m\in[n]
, by the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\big|\theta_{m}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\theta_{m}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbf{x})-\theta_{m}^{\gamma+1,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big|+\big|\theta_{m}^{\gamma+1,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\theta_{m}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbf{x})-\theta_{m}^{\gamma+1,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}+\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\theta_{m}^{\gamma+1,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|.

Together with Lemmas
B.2
and
B.3
, we finish the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
A.2
.

First, we consider the convergence property of the gradient descent algorithm under the RSC/RSS condition for
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
as specified in Lemma
B.6
. Applying Theorem 3.10 of
Bubeck (2015)
yields that for any
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
,

\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}\leq\zeta^{\Gamma}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{0}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2},

where
\zeta=1-\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+\frac{C}{n}}
. We then apply Lemma
B.7
and consider the case that
\bm{\theta}^{0}(\mathbf{x})=\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})
. Specifically, we have

\begin{split}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}&\leq\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\zeta^{\Gamma}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{0}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\zeta^{\Gamma}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}\\
&\lesssim\sqrt{n}\bigg(1-\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+\frac{C}{n}}\bigg)^{\Gamma}\leq\sqrt{n}\bigg(1-\widetilde{C}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)\bigg)^{\Gamma}.\end{split}

Here
\widetilde{C}
is some positive constant, and the last inequality holds as the term
\frac{C}{n}
dominates the denominator of
\frac{\lambda}{\lambda+\frac{C}{n}}
, given that
\lambda\asymp\frac{1}{n}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)
. In this case, a sufficiently large number of iterations
\Gamma
such that

\Gamma\geq\frac{\log\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)-\log\sqrt{n}}{\log\Big(1-\widetilde{C}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)\Big)}

(B.1)

guarantees that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{n}\bigg(1-\widetilde{C}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)\bigg)^{\Gamma}\leq h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

Then, we check condition (
B.1
). We consider the numerator and the denominator separately. We first consider the expansion of the denominator

\log\bigg(1-\widetilde{C}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)\bigg)=-\widetilde{C}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)+O\bigg(\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)^{2}\bigg),

(B.2)

and then compare (
B.2
) with the numerator
\log\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)\bigg)
. Here, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)^{2}\frac{\log\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)-\log\sqrt{n}}{\log\Big(1-\widetilde{C}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)\Big)}\xrightarrow{}0

\displaystyle\text{ since }\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)\xrightarrow{}0,

which guarantees the existence of some
\Gamma=\Omega\Bigg(\frac{\sqrt{n}}{h^{4}+\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Bigg)
that satisfies the condition (
B.1
).

Finally, by the inequality of the
\ell_{\infty}
-norm and the
\ell_{2}
-norm, we have

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}},

which completes the proof.
∎

B.1
Auxillary Lemmas for Leave-One-Out Technique

We provide the proofs for the inductive behavior of inequalities (
A.2
)-(
A.4
) for some index
\gamma\in[\Gamma]
, assuming that the inequalities (
A.1
)-(
A.4
) hold for
\gamma-1\in[\Gamma]
. We show the inductive behavior of (
A.1
) in Lemma
A.1
.

Before presenting the proofs, we first review the brief idea of the leave-one-out technique. This method involves updating the estimate
\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}
using the gradient of the leave-one-out objective function
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})
for
m\in[n]
, where the
m
-th entry in the objective function is replaced by its expected value. Specifically, the leave-one-out gradient
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})
is

\displaystyle\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E};i<j;i,j\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ij}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\bigg\{\Big(-y_{ij}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell})+\frac{\exp(\theta_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}{\exp{(\theta_{i}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}+\exp(\theta_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}\Big)\big(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}p\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\bigg\{\Big(-\frac{\exp(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}{\exp{(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}+\exp(\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{\exp(\theta_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}{\exp{(\theta_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}+\exp(\theta_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}\Big)\big(\mathbf{e}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)\bigg\}+\lambda\bm{\theta}.

In particular, the
m
-th entry of the gradient,
\big[\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})\big]_{m}
is

\displaystyle\big[\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})\big]_{m}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}p\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\Big(-\frac{\exp(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}{\exp{(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}+\exp(\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\exp(\theta_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}{\exp{(\theta_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}+\exp(\theta_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}\Big)+\lambda\theta_{m}.

Lemma B.1
.

Given that the inequalities (
A.1
)-(
A.4
) hold for some positive
\gamma
, there exists some positive constant
C
such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{n}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)

(B.3)

with a probability of
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Proof.

We start the proof for any
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
by modifying the gradient update rule such that

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})=\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\eta\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma};\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle\quad=\big\{\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\eta\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma};\mathbf{x})-[\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\eta\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})]\big\}-\eta\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle\quad=\Big\{\mathbf{I}_{n}-\eta\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\tau};\mathbf{x})d\tau\Big\}\big(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big)-\eta\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x}),

where
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\tau}=\bm{\theta}^{*}+\tau(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}-\bm{\theta}^{*})
. Here, the last equality holds as a consequence of the fundamental theorem of calculus
(
Lang, 1993
)
. Denote
\mathbf{A}
as the integration term by
\mathbf{A}=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\tau};\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}\tau
. By the triangle inequality, we have that

\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}\leq\underbrace{\big\|(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\eta\mathbf{A})(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x}))\big\|_{2}}_{\mathcal{M}_{1}}+\underbrace{\eta\big\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}}_{\mathcal{M}_{2}}.

(B.4)

We bound
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}
separately. First, we bound
\mathcal{M}_{1}
. We apply Lemma
B.9
and check the condition that
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\tau}(\mathbf{x})
belongs to a bound set centered at
\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})
. Let
\theta_{\max}=\max_{i}{\theta_{i}}
and
\theta_{\min}=\min_{i}{\theta_{i}}
. For
\bm{\theta}=\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\ldots,\theta_{n}\}
, we have

\widetilde{\theta}_{\tau,\max}(\mathbf{x})-\widetilde{\theta}_{\tau,\min}(\mathbf{x})\leq\theta_{\max}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\theta_{\min}^{*}(\mathbf{x})+2\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\leq\log\kappa+2\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty},

where
\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}=O(1)
by(
A.1
) given Assumption
4.1
holds. In this case, we apply Lemma
B.9
such that for any
\tau\in[0,1]
, there exist positive constants
c^{*}
and
C^{*}
such that

\lambda+\frac{c^{*}}{n}\leq\lambda_{\min,\perp}(\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\tau};\mathbf{x}))\leq\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\tau};\mathbf{x}))\leq\lambda+\frac{C^{*}}{n},

with a probability of
1-O(n^{-10})
.
For any matrix
A
, we let

\lambda_{\min,\perp}(A)=\min\left\{\mu\mid\mathbf{z}^{\top}A\mathbf{z}\geq\mu\|\mathbf{z}\|_{2}^{2}\text{ for all }\mathbf{z}\text{ with }\mathbf{1}^{\top}\mathbf{z}=0\right\},

namely, the smallest eigenvalue when restricted to vectors orthogonal to
\mathbf{1}
.

Given that
\mathbf{1}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{1}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})=0
according to Fact 1 in Section 6 of
Chen et al. (2019)
, with the difference being that we assume
\mathbf{1}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})=0
under the dynamic setup, we have that

\big\|(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\eta\mathbf{A})(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x}))\big\|_{2}\leq\max\big\{|1-\eta\lambda_{\min,\perp}(\mathbf{A})|,|1-\eta\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{A})|\big\}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}.

By
\eta\leq\frac{1}{\lambda+\frac{C^{*}}{n}}
in the setup, we have
1-\eta\lambda_{\min,\perp}(\mathbf{A})\geq 0
and
1-\eta\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{A})\geq 0
. Consequently,

\mathcal{M}_{1}=\big\|(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\eta\mathbf{A})(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x}))\big\|_{2}\leq(1-\eta\lambda_{\min,\perp}(\mathbf{A}))\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}.

Applying (
A.2
), we obtain

\mathcal{M}_{1}\leq C\sqrt{n}(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}})\text{ with probability }1-O(n^{-10}).

Then, we control
\mathcal{M}_{2}
. By the norm equivalence inequality
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\leq\|\cdot\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{n}\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
and Lemma
B.8
, we have, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\mathcal{M}_{2}\leq\eta\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}})\asymp\sqrt{n}(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}).

Plugging the results for
\mathcal{M}_{1}
and
\mathcal{M}_{2}
in (
B.4
), we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma B.2
.

Given that inequalities (
A.1
)-(
A.4
) hold for some positive real number
\gamma
, then there exists some positive constant
C
, such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big|\theta^{\gamma+1,(m)}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\leq C\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg),

with probability
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Proof.

This proof is mainly inspired by
Chen et al. (2019)
.
First, we apply the mean value theorem to the
m
-th component of
\bm{\theta}^{\gamma+1,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})
:

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\theta_{m}^{\gamma+1,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\theta_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})=\theta_{m}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\eta\Big[\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})\Big]_{m}-\theta_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\theta_{m}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\theta_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\eta\lambda\theta_{m}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\frac{\eta}{n^{2}pL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}p\mathcal{K}_{h}\big(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\big)\Bigg[-\frac{\exp\big(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\big)}{\exp\big(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\big)+\exp\big(\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\big)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{\exp\big(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\big)}{\exp\big(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\big)+\exp\big(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\big)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\big(1-\eta\lambda\big)\big(\theta_{m}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\theta_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big)}_{\mathcal{I}_{1}}-\underbrace{\eta\lambda\theta_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})}_{\mathcal{I}_{2}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\frac{\eta}{n^{2}pL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}p\mathcal{K}_{h}\big(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\big)\Bigg[\frac{\exp(c_{im})}{\big(1+\exp(c_{im})\big)^{2}}\times\Big[\big(\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\big)-\big(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\big)\Big]\Bigg]}_{\mathcal{I}_{3}},

where
c_{im}
lies within the interval between
\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})
and
\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})
. Here, the boundedness of
c_{im}
is guaranteed by (
B.11
).

Then, we bound
\mathcal{I}_{1}
,
\mathcal{I}_{2}
, and
\mathcal{I}_{3}
respectively. For
\mathcal{I}_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{2}
, we consider the setup in Algorithm
3
:
\eta\leq\frac{1}{\lambda+\frac{C^{*}}{n}}
for some constant
C^{*}
and
\lambda\asymp\frac{1}{n}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)
. The setup guarantees that

\eta\lambda=O\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)=o(1),

and it further implies that

\big|\mathcal{I}_{1}\big|\leq\big|\theta_{m}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\theta_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big|\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\quad\text{with probability }1-O(n^{-10}),

(B.5)

and

\big|\mathcal{I}_{2}\big|\leq\eta\lambda\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\leq\eta\lambda\log\kappa\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\quad\text{with probability }1-O(n^{-10}),

(B.6)

where
\kappa=O(1)
according to Assumption
4.5
.

For
\mathcal{I}_{3}
, we consider

\begin{split}&\Bigg|\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}p\mathcal{K}_{h}\big(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\big)\Bigg[\frac{\exp(c_{im})}{\big(1+\exp(c_{im})\big)^{2}}\Big[\big(\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\big)-\big(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\big)\Big]\Bigg]\Bigg|\\
&\overset{(i)}{\leq}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}p\mathcal{K}_{h}\big(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\big)\Bigg[\frac{2\exp(c_{im})}{\big(1+\exp(c_{im})\big)^{2}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big|\theta_{m}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\theta_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big|\Bigg]\\
&\overset{(ii)}{\lesssim}p\Bigg(\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}L_{im}\Bigg)\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big|\theta_{m}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\theta_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big|\\
&\overset{(iii)}{\lesssim}npL\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big),\end{split}

(B.7)

with probability
1-O(n^{-10})
. Here,
(i)
follows from the triangle inequality,
(ii)
follows from applying Lemma
D.5
on
\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}L_{im}
samples of
\mathcal{K}_{h}\big(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\big)
, and
(iii)
follows from (
A.3
) and Assumption
4.4
. By (
B.7
), we have that

\big|\mathcal{I}_{3}\big|\lesssim\eta\frac{npL\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)}{n^{2}pL}=O\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)\quad\text{with probability }1-O(n^{-10}).

(B.8)

Finally, combining (
B.5
),(
B.6
), and (
B.8
) completes the proof. The uniform boundedness is guaranteed by the arbitrariness of
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
and
m\in[n]
in the results for
\mathcal{I}_{1}
through
\mathcal{I}_{3}
.
∎

Lemma B.3
.

Given that the inequalities (
A.1
)-(
A.4
) hold for some positive
\gamma
, there exists some constant
C
, such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma+1,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}\leq C\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg),

with a probability of
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Proof.

We first consider the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{\gamma+1,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma+1}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\eta\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma};\mathbf{x})-\Big[\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\eta\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})\Big]

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\eta\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma};\mathbf{x})-\Big[\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\eta\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})\Big]

\displaystyle\quad-\eta\Big[\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})\Big]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\Big(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\eta\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\tau};\mathbf{x})d\tau\Big)\big(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big)}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\Big\{-\eta\Big[\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})-\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})\Big]\Big\}}_{\mathcal{U}_{2}}=\mathcal{U}_{1}+\mathcal{U}_{2}.

Then, we bound
\mathcal{U}_{1}
and
\mathcal{U}_{2}
. For
\mathcal{U}_{1}
, since (
A.4
) holds for some positive
\gamma
, we have

\big\|\mathcal{U}_{1}\big\|_{2}=\Big\|(\mathbf{I}_{n}-\eta\mathbf{A})\big(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big)\Big\|_{2}\leq\Big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\Big\|_{2}\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}},

(B.9)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. This approach is similar of bounding
\mathcal{M}_{1}
in Lemma
B.1
, with the difference being that we apply (
A.4
) instead of (
A.2
), and we omit the details here.

For
\mathcal{U}_{2}
, we calculate the closed form of the gradient
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})
and the leave-one-out gradient
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})
. We have the gradient
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})
:

\displaystyle\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ij}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\Big\{\Big(-y_{ij}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell})+\frac{\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}{\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))+\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}\Big)(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})\Big\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\underbrace{\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E};i<j;i,j\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ij}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\Big\{\Big(-y_{ij}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell})+\frac{\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}{\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))+\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}\Big)(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})\Big\}}_{\mathcal{N}_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\underbrace{\sum_{i\neq m,(i,m)\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\Big\{\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\frac{\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}{\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))+\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}\Big)(\mathbf{e}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{i})\Big\}}_{\mathcal{N}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\Big(\mathcal{N}_{1}+\mathcal{N}_{2}\Big).

Meanwhile, the leave-one-out gradient
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})
is

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{(m)}(\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)};\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\underbrace{\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E};i<j;i,j\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ij}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\Big\{\Big(-y_{ij}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell})+\frac{\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}{\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))+\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}\Big)(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})\Big\}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\underbrace{\begin{aligned} &\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}p\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\Big\{\Big(-\frac{\exp(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}{\exp(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))+\exp(\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}{\exp(\theta_{m}^{\gamma,(m)}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))+\exp(\theta_{i}^{\gamma,(m)}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}\Big)(\mathbf{e}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{i})\Big\}\end{aligned}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{1}+\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{2}\Big).

Combining the above two equalities together, we have

\big\|\mathcal{U}_{2}\big\|_{2}=\frac{\eta}{n^{2}pL}\big\|\mathcal{N}_{2}-\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{2}\big\|_{2}.

For
\big\|\mathcal{N}_{2}-\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{2}\big\|_{2}
, by Lemma
B.5
, we have that

\big\|\mathcal{N}_{2}-\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{2}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{np\log n}L\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}\Big),

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. Given the setup of Algorithm
3
such that
\eta\leq\min\Big\{\frac{1}{\lambda+\frac{C^{*}}{n}},1\Big\}
, we conclude that

\|\mathcal{U}_{2}\|_{2}\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

(B.10)

Combining (
B.9
) and (
B.10
) yields Lemma
B.3
. The uniformity is guaranteed since
\mathbf{x}
is arbitrarily selected in the region
\Omega=[0,1]^{d}
.
∎

Finally, we bound the distance between
\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\cdot)
and
\bm{\theta}^{*}(\cdot)
that we need in the proof for Lemma
B.2
. Specifically, we show that for some constant
C
,

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\leq C\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg).

(B.11)

Lemma B.4

(Bound on the Distance between
\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\cdot)
and
\bm{\theta}^{*}(\cdot)
)
.

Given that the inequalities (
A.1
)-(
A.4
) hold for some positive integer
\gamma
, then there exists some constant
C
such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\leq C_{1}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Proof.

The claim follows directly from inequalities (
A.1
) and (
A.4
). For any
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
and
m\in[n]
, the triangle inequality yields that

\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{1\leq m^{*}\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m^{*})}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}+\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}.

(B.12)

We next apply the upper bounds from (
A.1
) and (
A.4
) to the right-hand side of (
B.12
). We then complete the proof by taking the maximum over all
m\in[n]
and
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
on the left-hand side of (
B.12
).
∎

Lemma B.5

(Difference between
\mathcal{N}_{2}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{2}
)
.

Consider the same assumptions as in Lemma
B.3
. Define

\mathcal{N}_{2}=\sum_{i\neq m,(i,m)\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\bigg\{\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\frac{\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}{\exp{(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}+\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}\Big)\big(\mathbf{e}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)\bigg\},

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{2}=\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}p\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\bigg\{\Big(

\displaystyle-\frac{\exp(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}{\exp{(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}+\exp(\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}

\displaystyle+\frac{\exp(\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}{\exp{(\theta_{m}^{\gamma,(m)}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}+\exp(\theta_{i}^{\gamma,(m)}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))}\Big)\big(\mathbf{e}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)\bigg\}.

Then, we have

\big\|\mathcal{N}_{2}-\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{2}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{np\log n}L\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}\bigg)

for any
m\in[n]
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Proof.

The proof is inspired by the proof of Lemma 16 of
Chen et al. (2019)
. First, we have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{2}-\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{2}=\underbrace{\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\bigg\{\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\Big)\big(\mathbf{e}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)\bigg\}}_{\mathcal{J}_{1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{i\neq m}(p-\mathcal{E}_{im})\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\bigg\{\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}-\frac{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}\Big)\big(\mathbf{e}_{m}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)\bigg\}}_{\mathcal{J}_{2}},

where
\mathcal{E}_{ij}
indicates the existence of edge
(i,j)
for the Erdős–Rényi random graph
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{E})
. We denote the vectors
\mathcal{J}_{i}
, where
i\in\{1,2\}
, as
\mathcal{J}_{i}=\{j_{1}^{(i)},j_{2}^{(i)},\ldots,j_{n}^{(i)}\}
. We consider the
\ell_{2}
-norm of
\mathcal{J}_{i}
:

\big\|\mathcal{J}_{i}\big\|_{2}=\big|j_{m}^{(i)}\big|+\sqrt{\sum_{k:k\neq m;(k,m)\in\mathcal{E}}\big(j_{k}^{(i)}\big)^{2}},

where we evaluate
j_{m}^{(i)}
seperately as compared to
j_{k}^{(i)}
where
k\neq m
. The intuition comes from the leave-one-out technique, where the
m
-th coefficient contributes the most to the
\ell_{2}
-norm of
\mathcal{J}_{i}
. The idea is formalized in the following steps of deduction.

First, we bound
\mathcal{J}_{1}
. We discuss the entry of
\mathcal{J}_{1}=\big\{j_{1}^{(1)},j_{2}^{(1)},\ldots,j_{n}^{(1)}\big\}
by three different categories:

j^{(1)}_{k}=\begin{cases}&\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\Big),\ k=m.\\
&-\sum_{k\in[L_{km}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{k}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}-y_{km}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})\Big),\ k\neq m\text{ and }(k,m)\in\mathcal{E}.\\
&0,\ \text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then, we discuss the three categories separately. For
k\neq m
and
(k,m)\in\mathcal{E}
, we take the contraction over
O(L)
i.i.d. samples of
\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell}
. Specifically, we have that

\big|j_{k}^{(1)}\big|=L_{km}\bigg|\frac{1}{L_{km}}\sum_{k\in[L_{km}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{k}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}-y_{km}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})\Big)\bigg|.

By Assumption
4.4
, Lemma
D.3
, and Lemma
D.4
, we have

\sup_{k\neq m}\bigg|\frac{1}{L_{km}}\sum_{k\in[L_{km}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{k}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}-y_{km}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})\Big)\bigg|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}},

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

For
k\neq m
and
(k,m)\notin\mathcal{E}
, we have
j_{k}^{(1)}=0
.

For
k=m
, Lemma
D.5
implies that

\Bigg|\frac{\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\Big)}{\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}L_{im}}\Bigg|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}},

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

In conclusion, we have

\big\|\mathcal{J}_{1}\big\|_{2}=\big|j_{m}^{(1)}\big|+\sqrt{\sum_{\{k:k\neq m;(k,m)\in\mathcal{E}\}}\big(j_{k}^{(1)}\big)^{2}}\lesssim npL\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}+\sqrt{npL^{2}\cdot\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}},

(B.13)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. The right-hand side of (
B.13
) is dominated by

npL\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}+\sqrt{np}L\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}\lesssim L\sqrt{np\log{n}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}.

(B.14)

For
\mathcal{J}_{2}=\big\{j_{1}^{(2)},j_{2}^{(2)},\ldots,j_{n}^{(2)}\big\}
, we have

j^{(2)}_{k}=\begin{cases}&\sum_{i\neq m}(p-\mathcal{E}_{im})\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\bigg\{\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}-\frac{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})}}\Big)\bigg\},\ k=m.\\
&(1-p)\sum_{\ell\in[L_{km}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\bigg\{\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{k}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}-\frac{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{k}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}\Big)\bigg\},\ k\neq m,(k,m)\in\mathcal{E}.\\
&-p\sum_{\ell\in[L_{km}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\bigg\{\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{k}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}-\frac{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{k}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}\Big)\bigg\},\ \text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

By the mean value theorem between
\theta^{*}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})
and
\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{i}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\mathbf{x})
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{1}{L_{km}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{km}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell}

\displaystyle-\mathbf{x})\bigg\{\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{k}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}-\frac{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{k}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}\Big)\bigg\}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg|\frac{1}{L_{km}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{km}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\bigg|\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}.

Meanwhile, by Lemma
D.1
and Lemma
D.2
, we have that with a probability
1-O(n^{-11})
,

\bigg|\frac{1}{L_{km}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{km}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\bigg|\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}},

where the right-hand side term
h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}=o(1)
by Assumption
4.1
.

Furthermore, Assumption
4.3
guarantees that
\frac{1}{L_{km}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{km}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})=O(1)
. Consequently, for any
k\neq m
,

\begin{split}\bigg|\sum_{\ell\in[L_{km}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})&\bigg\{\Big(\frac{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{*}_{k}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}-\frac{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{m}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta^{\gamma,(m)}_{k}(\bm{X}_{km}^{\ell})}}\Big)\bigg|\\
&\lesssim L\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\text{ with probability }1-O(n^{-10}).\end{split}

Therefore, for
k\neq m
and
(k,m)\in\mathcal{E}
, we have

\big|j_{k}^{(2)}\big|\lesssim(1-p)L\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty},

(B.15)

and for
(k,m)\notin\mathcal{E}
, we have

\big|j_{k}^{(2)}\big|\lesssim pL\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty},

(B.16)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

For
k=m
, by Lemma
D.16
conditioning on
\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}
and setting
a=10
, we obtain

\big|j_{m}^{(2)}\big|\lesssim\sqrt{np\log n}L\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\text{ with probability }1-O(n^{-10}).

(B.17)

Finally, combining (
B.15
), (
B.16
), and (
B.17
), we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{J}_{2}\big\|_{2}\leq\big|j_{m}^{(2)}\big|+\sqrt{\sum_{\{k:k\neq m;(k,m)\in\mathcal{E}\}}(j_{k}^{(2)})^{2}+\sum_{\{k:k\neq m;(k,m)\notin\mathcal{E}\}}(j_{k}^{(2)})^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{np\log n}L\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}+\sqrt{np(1-p)L^{2}\big(\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{np\log n}L\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\text{ with probability }1-O(n^{-10}).

Given the upper bound of
\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\max_{1\leq m\leq n}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{\gamma,(m)}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}
as indicated by (
B.11
), we conclude that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, we have

\big\|\mathcal{N}_{2}-\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{2}\big\|_{2}\leq\big\|\mathcal{J}_{1}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\mathcal{J}_{2}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{np\log{n}}L\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}\Big),

which completes the proof.
∎

B.2
Auxillary Lemmas for proving Lemma
A.2

We present two auxiliary lemmas for proving Lemma
A.2
. First, we establish the Restrictive Strong Convexity (RSC) condition and the Restrictive Strong Smoothness (RSS) condition for
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})
for any
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
. We denote the Hessian matrix of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})
by
\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})
.

Lemma B.6

(RSC/RSS Condition for
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
)
.

Under Assumptions
4.1
to
4.10
, the regularized likelihood function
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x})
for
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
and
\bm{\theta}(\mathbf{x})\in\Theta(C_{0})=\{\bm{\theta}:\inf_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\Omega}\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\|\leq C_{0}\}
with some positive
C_{0}
, satisfies the RSC/RSS condition that

\lambda\leq\inf_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta}\lambda_{\min,\perp}(\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x}))\leq\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x}))\leq\frac{C}{n}+\lambda,

(B.18)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. Here,
C
is a positive constant determined by the maximal/minimal values of all
L_{ij}
where
(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}
, and the upper/lower bounds for
f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})
as
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
.

Proof.

The first inequality in (
B.18
) holds by the strong convexity of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
guaranteed by the regularization term as
\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})\succeq\lambda\mathbf{I}_{n}
for any
\bm{\theta}
. We then apply Lemma
B.9
and complete the proof.
∎

Furthermore, we bound the distance between the true value
\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})
and the regularized MLE
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})
in Lemma
B.7
.

Lemma B.7

(Distance between
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})
and
\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})
)
.

There exists some positive constant
C
such that
\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}\leq C\sqrt{n}
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Proof.

First, we consider the Taylor expansion of
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\widehat{\bm{\theta}},\mathbf{x})
that

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\widehat{\bm{\theta}},\mathbf{x})=\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{x})+\big\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x}),\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big)^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}};\mathbf{x})\big(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big),

where
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}
lies between
\widehat{\bm{\theta}}
and
\bm{\theta}^{*}
. Since
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\widehat{\bm{\theta}},\mathbf{x})\leq\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{x})
, we have

\big\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x}),\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big)^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}};\mathbf{x})\big(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big)\big\rangle\leq 0.

By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\frac{1}{2}\big(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big)^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}};\mathbf{x})\big(\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big)\leq\big\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}\big\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}.

(B.19)

Therefore, we derive

\begin{split}\big\|\widehat{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{x})-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}&\leq\frac{2\big\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}}{\min_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\lambda_{\min,\perp}\big(\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}};\mathbf{x})\big)}\\
&\overset{(i)}{\leq}\frac{2\big\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})\big\|_{2}}{\lambda}\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\frac{2\sqrt{n}\big\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}}{\lambda}\overset{(iii)}{\lesssim}\frac{h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}}{\sqrt{n}\lambda},\end{split}

(B.20)

where
(i)
holds as
\mathbf{H}_{\lambda}(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}};\mathbf{x})\succeq\lambda\mathbf{I}_{n}
,
(ii)
holds by the norm equivalence inequality
\|\cdot\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{n}\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
, and
(iii)
holds as we apply Lemma
B.8
.

Finally, we set
\lambda\asymp\frac{1}{n}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)
to ensure the uniformly of (
B.20
) for any
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
, which completes the proof.
∎

B.3
Proof for the Bound on the Gradient

We discuss the behavior of the gradient
\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})
that

\displaystyle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})

(B.21)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ij}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\bigg\{\Big(-y_{ij}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell})+\frac{\exp(\theta^{*}_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}{\exp{(\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}+\exp(\theta^{*}_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}\Big)\big(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)\bigg\}.

Lemma B.8

(Bound on the Gradient on
\bm{\theta}^{*}
)
.

Given the conditions in Section
4.1
, there exists a positive constant
C
such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{C}{n}\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}},

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. Furthermore, if
\lambda\asymp\frac{1}{n}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)
, there exists a positive constant
C^{*}
such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{C^{*}}{n}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)

(B.22)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Proof.

To facilitate our discussion, we let

\bm{A}=\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ij}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\bigg\{\Big(-y_{ij}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell})+\frac{\exp{(\theta^{*}_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}}{\exp{(\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}+\exp{(\theta^{*}_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}}\Big)\big(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)\bigg\},

and denote the
k^{\text{th}}
entry of the gradient
\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})
as
A_{k}
:

A_{k}=\sum_{(i,k)\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ik}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{ik}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\bigg\{-y_{ik}(\bm{X}_{ik}^{\ell})+\frac{e^{\theta_{k}^{*}(\bm{X}_{ik}^{\ell})}}{e^{\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{ik}^{\ell})}+e^{\theta_{k}^{*}(\bm{X}_{ik}^{\ell})}}\bigg\}.

Consider the residuals
\epsilon_{ik}^{\ell}
that

\epsilon^{\ell}_{ik}=-y_{ik}(\bm{X}_{ik}^{\ell})+\frac{e^{\theta_{k}^{*}}(\bm{X}_{ik}^{\ell})}{e^{\theta_{i}^{*}}(\bm{X}_{ik}^{\ell})+e^{\theta_{k}^{*}}(\bm{X}_{ik}^{\ell})}.

We observe that the residuals
\epsilon_{ik}^{\ell}
are independent across all pairs
(i,k)\in\mathcal{E}
due to the independence of
\bm{X}_{ik}^{\ell}
’s, as ensured by Assumption
4.2
. Furthermore, these residuals are uniformly bounded that
|\epsilon_{ik}^{\ell}|\leq 2
, and their mean is zero, as specified by the model setup. Consequently, the conditions listed in Section
D.1
are satisfied, and the mean-variance decomposition in Lemma
D.5
is applicable for the proof. Specifically, we have

\sup_{k\in[n]}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|A_{k}\big|=O\bigg(npL\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg),\text{ with probability at least}1-O(n^{-10}).

It further implies that for any
k\in[n]
,

\begin{split}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})\big\|_{\infty}=\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\sup_{k\in[n]}&\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|A_{k}\big|\lesssim\frac{npL\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)}{n^{2}pL}=\frac{1}{n}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg),\end{split}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. For the regularized gradient
\nabla\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}(\bm{\theta}^{*};\mathbf{x})
, (
B.22
) holds when
\lambda\asymp\frac{1}{n}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)
.

Finally, applying the triangle inequality completes the proof.
∎

B.4
Proof for the Bound on the Hessian Matrix

We derive bounds for the eigenvalues of Hessian matrix
\mathbf{H}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})
given by

\mathbf{H}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{n^{2}pL}\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ij}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\bigg\{\frac{\exp(\theta_{i}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))\exp(\theta_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}{(\exp(\theta_{i}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))+\exp(\theta_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell})))^{2}}\big(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)\big(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}\bigg\}.

(B.23)

We introduce the matrix
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathcal{G}}=\mathbf{Q}_{\mathcal{G(V,E)}}
associated with the Erdős–Rényi random graph
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{G(V,E)}
, where
|\mathcal{V}|=n
, and each edge is included with probability
p
. We define
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathcal{G}}
as

\mathbf{Q}_{\mathcal{G}}=\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}.

Lemma B.9

(Bound on the Eigenvalues of the Hessian Matrix)
.

For any
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
and positive constant
C_{0}
such that
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta(C_{0})=\big\{\bm{\theta}:\inf_{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\Omega}\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}\big\}
, there exist positive constants
0<C_{\min{}}\leq C_{\max{}}<\infty
such that the following holds uniformly with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
:

\inf_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\inf_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta}\lambda_{\min,\perp}\big(\mathbf{H}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})\big)\geq\frac{C_{\min{}}}{n}\text{\ \ \ and \ \ }\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\sup_{\bm{\theta}\in\Theta}\lambda_{\max}\big(\mathbf{H}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})\big)\leq\frac{C_{\max{}}}{n}.

Here
\lambda_{\min{},\perp}
denotes the smallest eigenvalue that is restricted to vectors orthogonal to
\mathbf{1}
.

Proof.

Given the existence of a positive constant
C_{0}
such that
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta(C_{0})
, Lemma
B.10
shows that for any
\bm{\theta}\in\Theta(C_{0})
and
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
,

\frac{\exp(\theta_{i}(\mathbf{x}))\exp(\theta_{j}(\mathbf{x}))}{\big(\exp(\theta_{i}(\mathbf{x}))+\exp(\theta_{j}(\mathbf{x}))\big)^{2}}\in\bigg[\frac{1}{4\kappa e^{2C_{0}}},\frac{1}{4}\bigg].

We define matrices
\mathbf{M}(\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x})
and
\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{x})
as

\mathbf{M}(\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x})=\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ij}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)\bigg\{\frac{\exp(\theta_{i}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))\exp(\theta_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))}{(\exp(\theta_{i}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}))+\exp(\theta_{j}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell})))^{2}}\big(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)\big(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i}\big)^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}\bigg\},

and

\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ij}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}\Big(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}\Big)(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}.

Since Weyl’s inequality implies

\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{M}(\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}))\leq\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{x}));\ \ \lambda_{\min,\perp}(\mathbf{M}(\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}))\geq\frac{1}{4\kappa e^{2C_{0}}}\lambda_{\min,\perp}(\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{x})),

bounding the eigenvalues of
\mathbf{M}(\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x})
defined in (
B.23
) suffices to bound the eigenvalues of
\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{x})
.

First, we bound
\lambda_{\min,\perp}(\mathbf{M}(\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}))
. We have the following decomposition of

\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{x})
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{x})&=\underbrace{\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}L_{ij}f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}}_{\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{x})}-\underbrace{\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\Big(L_{ij}f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})-\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ij}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big)(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}}_{\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{x})}.\end{split}

By Weyl’s inequality, we have
\lambda_{\min,\perp}(\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{x}))\geq\lambda_{\min,\perp}(\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{x}))-\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{x}))
.

For
\lambda_{\min,\perp}(\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{x}))
, Lemma
B.11
implies that with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\begin{split}\lambda_{\min{},\perp}(\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{x}))\geq L_{m}f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\lambda_{\min{},\perp}\Big(\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}\Big)\geq\frac{npL_{m}f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})}{2}.\end{split}

(B.24)

For
\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{x}))
, we consider
\mathcal{D}_{ij}=\Big|\frac{1}{L_{ij}}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{ij}]}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{ij}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\Big|
. Applying the union bound among
(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}
, we have

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}_{ij}\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}\bigg)=\mathbb{P}\bigg(\cap_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\Big\{\mathcal{D}_{ij}\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}\Big\}\bigg)\\
&=1-\mathbb{P}\bigg(\cup_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\Big\{\mathcal{D}_{ij}\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}\Big\}^{c}\bigg)\\
&\geq 1-\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\{\mathcal{D}_{ij}\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}\Big\}^{c}\Big)\\
&=1-\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\bigg\{1-\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\{\mathcal{D}_{ij}\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}\Big\}\Big)\bigg\}.\end{split}

(B.25)

By applying Lemma
D.1
and Lemma
D.2
with
\tau=12
, we have for any
(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}
,

\mathbb{P}\Big(\Big\{\mathcal{D}_{ij}\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}\Big\}\Big)=1-O(n^{-12}).

Combining the results with (
B.25
) and considering the graph structure
|\mathcal{E}|\lesssim n^{2}
, we obtain

\sup_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}_{ij}\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{Lh^{d}}}\text{ with probability at least }1-O(n^{-10}).

Given Assumption
4.1
, we conclude that
\sup_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}\mathcal{D}_{ij}=o(1)
with probability
1-O(n^{-10})
. Lemma
B.11
implies that, with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, the difference of each pair is bounded as

\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{x}))=\lambda_{\max}\bigg(\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}L_{ij}\mathcal{D}_{ij}(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}\bigg)=o(npL).

(B.26)

Combining (
B.24
) and (
B.26
) guarantees the existence of some
C_{\min{}}>0
, such that

\lambda_{\min{},\perp}(\mathbf{M}(\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}))\geq\frac{1}{4\kappa e^{2C_{0}}}\big(\lambda_{\min{},\perp}(\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{x}))-\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{x}))\big)\geq C_{\min{}}npL

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. Therefore, by (
B.23
), we have

\lambda_{\min{},\perp}\big(\mathbf{H}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x})\big)\geq\frac{C_{\min{}}npL}{n^{2}pL}=\frac{C_{\min{}}}{n},\text{ with probability at least }1-O(n^{-10}).

Then, we bound
\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{M}(\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}))
. Consider
\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{x}))
, and Weyl’s inequality implies that

\lambda_{\max{}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{x}))\leq\lambda_{\max{}}(\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{x}))+\lambda_{\max{}}(\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{x})).

For
\lambda_{\max{}}(\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{x}))
, Lemma
B.11
implies that

\lambda_{\max{}}(\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{x}))\leq L_{M}f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\lambda_{\max{}}\bigg(\sum_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{E}}(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})(\mathbf{e}_{j}-\mathbf{e}_{i})^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}\bigg)\leq 2npL_{M}f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x}),

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. Combining the upper bound of
\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{x}))
and
\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{x}))
from (
B.26
), we have

\lambda_{\max{}}(\mathbf{M}(\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}))\leq\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{\max{}}(\mathbf{P}(\mathbf{x}))\leq\frac{1}{4}\big(\lambda_{\max{}}(\mathbf{N}(\mathbf{x}))+\lambda_{\max{}}(\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{x}))\big)\leq C_{\max{}}npL,

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. Consequently, we have

\lambda_{\max{}}(\mathbf{H}(\bm{\theta};\mathbf{x}))\leq\frac{C_{\max{}}npL}{n^{2}pL}=\frac{C_{\max{}}}{n},\text{ with probability at least}1-O(n^{-10}),

which completes the proof.
∎

The auxiliary Lemmas
B.10
and
B.11
provide key technical insights essential for the proof of Lemma
B.9
.

Lemma B.10

(Constraint on Function Value)
.

Suppose that Assumption
4.5
holds. For the function
g(\cdot,\cdot):\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
,

g(x_{1},x_{2})=\frac{e^{x_{1}}e^{x_{2}}}{(e^{x_{1}}+e^{x_{2}})^{2}},

(B.27)

we have

\frac{1}{4\kappa e^{2C}}\leq\min_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\{g(\theta_{i},\theta_{j})\}\leq\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\{g(\theta_{i},\theta_{j})\}\leq\frac{1}{4}

for all
\bm{\theta}=\{\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\ldots,\theta_{n}\}^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{\infty}\leq C
. Here
\kappa
is the the ratio of the upper bound to the lower bound of the preference scores.

Proof.

First, we have that

\max_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\{g(\theta_{i},\theta_{j})\}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

Therefore, it suffices to show that

\min_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\{g(\theta_{i},\theta_{j})\}\geq\frac{1}{4\kappa e^{2C}}.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\theta_{i}\leq\theta_{j}
. Therefore, we have that

\frac{e^{\theta_{i}}e^{\theta_{j}}}{(e^{\theta_{i}}+e^{\theta_{j}})^{2}}=\frac{e^{\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}}}{(1+e^{\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}})^{2}}=\frac{e^{-|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|}}{(1+e^{-|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|})^{2}}\geq\frac{e^{-|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|}}{4},

as
e^{-|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|}>0
and
(1+e^{-|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|})^{2}\leq 4
. Furthermore, we have that

|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|\leq|\theta_{i}-\theta^{*}_{i}|+|\theta^{*}_{i}-\theta^{*}_{j}|+|\theta^{*}_{j}-\theta_{j}|\leq 2C+\log\kappa,

which yields that for any
(\theta_{i},\theta_{j})
pairs,

g(\theta_{i},\theta_{j})\geq\frac{e^{-|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}|}}{4}\geq\frac{e^{-(2C+\log\kappa)}}{4}=\frac{1}{4\kappa e^{2C}}.

(B.28)

Here (
B.28
) completes the proof by the arbitrarity of pair
(\theta_{i},\theta_{j})
.

∎

Recall that
\lambda_{\min{},\perp}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathcal{G}})
is the spectral gap of the Laplacian matrix of the Erdős–Rényi random graph
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{E})
. The following lemma is Lemma 10 of
Chen et al. (2019)
, as is a direct corollary of (5.3.3) in
Tropp (2015)
. We provide it here fore self-completeness.

Lemma B.11

(Bound on
\lambda_{\max}
and
\lambda_{\min{},\perp}
for
\mathbf{Q}_{\mathcal{G}}
)
.

For any
\tau>0
, there exists some positive constant
C=C(\tau)
such that for probability
p
satisfying Assumption
4.1
,

\mathbb{P}\big(np/2\leq\lambda_{\min{},\perp}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathcal{G}})\leq\lambda_{\max{}}(\mathbf{Q}_{\mathcal{G}})\leq 2np\big)\geq 1-O(n^{-\tau}).

Appendix C
Proof of Lemmas in Section
A.3

Lemma C.1

(Selection of
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
for
|T-T_{0}|
)
.

Consider
T
defined in (
3.3
) and
T_{0}
defined in (
A.27
). Given Assumptions
4.2
–
4.5
and
4.8
–
4.10
, there exist
\zeta_{1}=o\Big(\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}\Big)
and
\zeta_{2}=O(n^{-10})
such that

\mathbb{P}\big(|T-T_{0}|>\zeta_{1}\big)<\zeta_{2},

where
h
satisfies
npLh^{d+4}\leq\log(nh^{d/2-1})
.

Proof.

Let
T^{m}(\mathbf{x})=\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\big|\widehat{\theta}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\theta^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|.

We have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}}T^{m}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=(1+\epsilon_{1,m}(\mathbf{x}))\bigg|-\frac{\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|+\epsilon_{2,m}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle\leq(1+\epsilon_{1,m}(\mathbf{x}))\frac{1}{\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}}T_{0}^{m}(\mathbf{x})+\epsilon_{2,m}(\mathbf{x})+(1+\epsilon_{1,m}(\mathbf{x}))\bigg|\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\frac{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|.

Denote

\epsilon_{3,m}(\mathbf{x})=(1+\epsilon_{1,m}(\mathbf{x}))\bigg|\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\frac{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|,

and we have

T^{m}(\mathbf{x})-T_{0}^{m}(\mathbf{x})=\epsilon_{1,m}(\mathbf{x})T_{0}^{m}(\mathbf{x})+\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}(\epsilon_{2,m}(\mathbf{x})+\epsilon_{3,m}(\mathbf{x})).

Then, we bound each part separately. For
\epsilon_{3,m}(\mathbf{x})
, Lemma
C.3
indicates that
\big|\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|
is bounded above by
O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)
. Lemma
C.3
and Lemma
C.4
imply there exist
C_{1},C_{2}
such that
\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\geq C_{1}
, and
\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\geq C_{2}
with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. Furthermore, Lemma
D.4
on
\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})
yields that

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\big|\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

Therefore, we have

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\mathcal{U}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\frac{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|\lesssim\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
. By Lemma
A.3
,
\|\epsilon_{1}\|_{\infty}=o(1)
and we have

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\epsilon_{3,m}(\mathbf{x})\lesssim\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}.

(C.1)

For
\epsilon_{2,m}(\mathbf{x})
, by Lemma
A.3
, we have

\|\epsilon_{2}\|_{\infty}=o\bigg(dh^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)

(C.2)

Combining (
C.1
) and (
C.2
), we have with probability
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Big(T^{m}(\mathbf{x})-T_{0}^{m}(\mathbf{x})\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{npLh^{d}}\bigg[o\Big(dh^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)+\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\lesssim o\Big(\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}\Big),

where
h
satisfies
npLh^{d+4}\leq\log(nh^{d/2-1})
.

Therefore, there exists
\zeta_{1}=o\Big(\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}\Big)
and
\zeta_{2}=O(n^{-10})
such that

\mathbb{P}(|\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}T^{m}(\mathbf{x})-\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}T_{0}^{m}(\mathbf{x})|=\mathbb{P}(|T-T_{0}|>\zeta_{1})<\zeta_{2},

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.2

(Selection of
\zeta_{1}
and
\zeta_{2}
for
|W-W_{0}|
)
.

Consider
W
and
W_{0}
defined in (
3.4
) and (
A.28
). Given Assumptions
4.2
–
4.5
and
4.8
–
4.10
, there exist
\zeta_{1}=O(n^{-\epsilon/4}\sqrt{\log n})
and
\zeta_{2}=O(n^{-10})
such that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathbb{P}\big(|W-W_{0}|>\zeta_{1}\big|\{y_{im},\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\}_{i\neq m,\ell\in[L_{im}]}\big)>\zeta_{2}\Big)<\zeta_{2},

where
h\leq({npLd^{2}})^{-\frac{1}{d+4}}
.

Proof.

Let

\mathcal{A}_{1}=\bigg\{\frac{np}{2}\leq\min_{i\in[n]}d_{i}\leq\max_{i\in[n]}d_{i}\leq\frac{3np}{2}\bigg\},

where
d_{i}=\sum_{j\neq i;j\in[n]}\mathcal{E}_{ij}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(|W-W_{0}|>\zeta_{1}\big|\mathcal{D}\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(|W-W_{0}|>\zeta_{1}\big|\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{D}\Big)\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{1}\big|\mathcal{D}\big)+\mathbb{P}\Big(|W-W_{0}|>\zeta_{1}\big|\mathcal{A}_{1}^{c},\mathcal{D}\Big)\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{1}^{c}\big|\mathcal{D}\big).

By Lemma
D.13
,
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{1}^{c}\big)\gtrsim O(n^{-10})
. Therefore, it suffices to show that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathbb{P}\big(|W-W_{0}|>\zeta_{1}\big|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\big)>\zeta_{2}\Big)<\zeta_{2}\ \ \text{for some}\ \ \zeta_{1}\text{ and }\zeta_{2},

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}=\{\mathcal{A}_{1},\mathcal{D}\}
.

First, we have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\big|W_{0}-W\big|

\displaystyle\leq\bigg|\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\bigg|\frac{\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|-\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\bigg|\frac{\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\bigg|\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\bigg|\frac{\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|-\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\bigg|\frac{\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\bigg|\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|

\displaystyle\quad+\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}}{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\Big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\Big)\bigg|.

Then, we bound the three parts separately.

Part I:
Consider

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\bigg|.

The main idea of the proof is to apply the Borell-TIS inequality
(
Adler and Taylor, 2009
)
. For the validity of the inequality, we want to obtain the upper bound of
\Var\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\big)
and the conditional expectation of
