\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\bigg|
.

First, we consider the variance. Note that
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}]=0
, and

\displaystyle\Var\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi\big(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\big)\Big)\bigg)^{2},

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}^{2}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}h^{2d}}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}^{2}\Big(\frac{\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}}{h}\Big).

By Assumptions
4.8
–
4.10
, we consider
\mathcal{K}^{2}(\cdot)
as a new kernel and denote
C_{\text{square}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{K}^{2}(u)du
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Var\bigg(\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C_{\text{square}}\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\frac{\Xi}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}h^{d}C_{\text{square}}}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}^{2}\Big(\frac{\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}}{h}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\big(\Xi\big)^{2}\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\Xi}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\Big\{\frac{1}{h^{d}C_{\text{square}}}\mathcal{K}^{2}\Big(\frac{\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}}{h}\Big)\Big\}\bigg|.

First, by Lemma
D.13
, we have with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})

\displaystyle\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\big(\Xi\big)^{2}\lesssim(npL)^{2}.

We apply Lemma
D.1
and Lemma
D.2
on the new kernel
\mathcal{K}^{2}(\cdot)/C_{\text{square}}
. By Lemma
D.13
, we have with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\Xi}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\Big\{\frac{1}{h^{d}C_{\text{square}}}\mathcal{K}^{2}\Big(\frac{\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}}{h}\Big)\Big\}-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\bigg|\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

By Assumption
4.3
on the bound of
f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})
, we have that for sufficiently large
n

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Var\bigg(\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\Big|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\bigg)\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\ \text{ with probability}\ 1-O(n^{-10}),

(C.3)

which completes the proof for obtaining the upper bound of the variance.

Then, we consider the conditional expectation:

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{m\in[n],t\in[0,1]}\Big|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\Big|\bigg|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\bigg]\\
=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{m\in[n],t\in[0,1]}\Big|\sqrt{h\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}L_{im}}&\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\xi_{im}^{\ell}\mathcal{E}_{im}K_{h}(T_{im}^{\ell}-t)\\
&\times\Big(-y_{im}(T_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\widehat{\theta}_{m}(T_{im}^{\ell})-\widehat{\theta}_{i}(T_{im}^{\ell}))\Big)\Big|\bigg|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\bigg].\end{split}

(C.4)

To derive the upper bound of the conditional expectation, we apply Dudley’s entropy integral for
\xi_{im}^{\ell}
in
\mathcal{G}^{*}(\mathbf{x})
conditioning on
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}
.
Let
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{i,m}
be a probability measure considering the indicators
\delta_{im}^{\ell}
as Dirac measure such that

\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{i,m}=\frac{1}{\Xi}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\delta_{im}^{\ell},

where
\delta_{im}^{\ell}
is the indicator for the respective data
\mathcal{D}_{im}^{\ell}=\big\{y_{im},\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\big\}
.
We denote the function class
\mathcal{H}_{h}
by

\mathcal{H}_{h}=\Big\{\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\widehat{\theta}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\big)\Big|i,m\in[n],\ell\in[L_{im}],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}\Big\}.

Given
\xi_{im}^{\ell}
being the only randomness in (
C.4
) with guaranteed sub-Gaussianity, we have that

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Big|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\Big|\Big|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\bigg]\lesssim\sqrt{npLh^{d}}\ \mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\sigma_{i,m}}\sqrt{\log N\big(\mathcal{H}_{h},L^{2}({\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{i,m}}),\epsilon\big)}\mathrm{d}\epsilon\Big|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}\bigg].\end{split}

(C.5)

We then bound the covering number,
N\big(\mathcal{H}_{h},L^{2}({\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{i,m}}),\epsilon\big)
. Denote

\sigma_{i,m}^{2}=\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{i,m}\bigg[\Big(\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\big(-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\widehat{\theta}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\big)\Big)^{2}\bigg].

Similar to (
C.3
), we have
\sigma_{i,m}^{2}=O(1/h^{d})
.
By
-y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})+\psi(\widehat{\theta}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\in[-1,1]
, the covering number of
\mathcal{H}_{h}
is bounded above such that

N\{\mathcal{H}_{h},L^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{i,m}),\epsilon\}\leq\sup_{\mathcal{Q}}\big\{N\{\mathcal{H}_{h},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\epsilon\}\big\}\leq\bigg(\frac{2C_{\mathcal{K}}\|\mathcal{K}\|_{\text{TV}}}{h\epsilon}\bigg)^{4},

(C.6)

where
\mathcal{Q}
is any probability measure.

Therefore, by (
C.6
), we conclude that

\int_{0}^{\sigma_{i,m}}\sqrt{\log N\big(\mathcal{H}_{h},L^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{i,m}),\epsilon\big)}\mathrm{d}\epsilon\leq\int_{0}^{\sigma_{i,m}}2\sqrt{\log\big(2C_{\mathcal{K}}\|\mathcal{K}\|_{\text{TV}}\big)-\log\big(h\epsilon\big)}\mathrm{d}\epsilon.

By Jensen’s inequality and the concavity of
\varphi(\cdot)=\sqrt{\cdot}
, we further have

\int_{0}^{\sigma_{i,m}}\sqrt{\log N\big(\mathcal{H}_{h},L^{2}(\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{i,m}),\epsilon\big)}\mathrm{d}\epsilon\leq 2\sqrt{\int_{0}^{\sigma_{i,m}}\Big(\log\big(2C_{\mathcal{K}}\|\mathcal{K}\|_{\text{TV}}\big)-\log\big(h\epsilon\big)\Big)\mathrm{d}\epsilon.}

(C.7)

Integrating out the RHS of (
C.7
) gives

\int_{0}^{\sigma_{P}}\sqrt{\log N\big(\mathcal{H}_{h},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\epsilon\big)}\mathrm{d}\epsilon\leq\sqrt{\sigma_{P}\big(\log(2C_{\mathcal{K}}\|\mathcal{K}\|_{\text{TV}})+1-\log(h\sigma_{P})\big)},

where we apply the finite maximal inequality given the sub-Gaussianity. Combining with (
C.5
), we directly have the desired upper bound of the conditional expectation.

Finally, we apply Borell-TIS inequality
(
Adler and Taylor, 2009
)
on
\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Big|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\Big|
conditioning on all
\mathcal{E}_{im}
,
\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}
, and
y_{im}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})
. In particular, we have that for any
u>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\bigg(&\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Big|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\Big|\geq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Big|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\Big|\bigg|\Big\{y_{im},\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\Big\}\bigg]+u\bigg|\Big\{y_{im},\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\Big\}\bigg)\\
&\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Var\Big(\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\big\{y_{im},\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\big\}\Big)}\bigg)\\
&\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{u^{2}}{2\big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\big)}\bigg).\end{split}

(C.8)

Applying (
C.3
) and (
C.4
) to (
C.8
), we have that there exists some
u=O(\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})})
, such that

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Big|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\Big|\gtrsim\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}\bigg|y_{im},\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\bigg)\leq\zeta_{2}

(C.9)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
, where
\zeta_{2}=O(n^{-10})
.

Part II:
Consider

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Big|\frac{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\Big|.

We have the following decomposition:

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|\leq\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|+\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|,

where

\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{npL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\big(\psi^{\prime}(\widehat{\theta}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))-\psi^{\prime}(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\big).

We then bound these parts separately. For
\big|\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|
, by Theorem
4.3
and Lipschitz property of
\psi^{\prime}(\cdot)
, we have that

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\big|\psi^{\prime}(\widehat{\theta}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))-\psi^{\prime}(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\big|\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

(C.10)

By Lemmas
D.1
and
D.2
and (
C.10
), we have

\begin{split}&\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\big|\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\\
&\quad=\frac{1}{npL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(T_{im}^{\ell}-t)\big(\psi^{\prime}(\widehat{\theta}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))-\psi^{\prime}(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\big)\\
&\quad\lesssim\frac{1}{npL}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(T_{im}^{\ell}-t)\\
&\quad\lesssim\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)^{2}.\end{split}

(C.11)

For
\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})
, we first have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{V}_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x}))^{2}-\mathcal{V}_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})(\mathcal{V}_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})).

By Lemma
C.4
,
\mathcal{V}_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\gtrsim 1.
By Lemma
C.3
, we have

\sup_{m\in n,\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

(C.12)

Combining (
C.11
), (
C.12
), we obtain

\sup_{m\in n,\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

(C.13)

Then, by Lemma
D.1
and Lemma
D.2
, we further have

\mathcal{V}_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})(\mathcal{V}_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x}))\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

(C.14)

Therefore, by (
C.11
) and (
C.14
), we conclude that

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

Similarly, we have

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

Combining the two inequalities above, we conclude that

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}

(C.15)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Hence, we have with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\bigg(&\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\cdot\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\bigg|\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}{\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\bigg|\\
&\quad\gtrsim\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}\big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\big)\bigg|y_{im},\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\bigg)\leq\zeta_{2},\end{split}

where
\zeta_{2}=O(n^{-10})
.

Part III:
Consider

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}}{\overline{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\Big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\Big)\bigg|,

where

\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})=\frac{1}{npL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\xi_{im}^{\ell}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big[\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))-\psi(\widehat{\theta}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big].

Similarly, we have
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\big\{y_{im},\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\big\}\big]=0
, and

\displaystyle\Var\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\big)

\displaystyle\ \ =\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\bigg(\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big(\psi(\theta^{*}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta^{*}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))-\psi(\widehat{\theta}_{m}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\widehat{\theta}_{i}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big)\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\ \ \lesssim\frac{1}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)^{2}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}^{2}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}).

Therefore, we have that

\begin{split}&\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Var\bigg(\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)\Big|\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\bigg)\\
&\ \ \leq\frac{C_{\text{square}}}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)^{2}\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\frac{\Xi}{h^{d}C_{\text{square}}}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}^{2}\Big(\frac{\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}}{h}\Big)\\
&\ \ \leq\frac{C_{\text{square}}}{n^{2}p^{2}L^{2}}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)^{2}\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\big(\Xi\big)^{2}\cdot\\
&\quad\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{1}{\Xi}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\Big\{\frac{1}{h^{d}C_{\text{square}}}\mathcal{K}^{2}\Big(\frac{\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x}}{h}\Big)\Big\}\bigg|\\
&\ \ {\lesssim}\bigg(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\bigg)^{3},\ \text{ with probability }1-O(n^{-10}).\end{split}

(C.16)

By the finite maximal inequality, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}&\Big|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)\Big|\bigg|\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\bigg]\\
&\lesssim\sqrt{\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Var\Big(\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)\big|\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\Big)}\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}.\end{split}

(C.17)

We then apply Borell-TIS inequality
(
Adler and Taylor, 2009
)
for
\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)
conditional on
\mathcal{E}_{im}
and
\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}
. Consequently, we have that for any
u>0
,

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\bigg(&\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Big|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\bigg)\Big|\\
&\quad\geq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Big|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)\Big|\bigg|\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\bigg]+u\bigg|\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\bigg)\\
&\leq\exp\bigg(-\frac{u^{2}}{2\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Var\Big(\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)\big|\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\Big)}\bigg).\end{split}

By (
C.16
) and (
C.17
), we have that there exists some

u=O\bigg(\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)\bigg),

such that

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Big|\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)\Big|\gtrsim\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)\bigg|\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\bigg)\leq\zeta^{\prime}_{2}

(C.18)

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
and
\zeta^{\prime}_{2}=O(n^{-10})
. By
\widetilde{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})=O(1)
, we further have with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
,

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Big|\frac{\sqrt{h^{d}\Xi}}{\widetilde{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})}\big(\mathcal{G}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)\Big|\gtrsim\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)\bigg|\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\bigg)\leq\zeta^{\prime}_{2},

(C.19)

which completes the proof for part III.

Combining (
C.9
), (
C.15
), and (
C.19
) shows the existence of some
\zeta_{1}=o(\sqrt{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})})
and
\zeta_{2}=O(n^{-10})
such that

\mathbb{P}\Big(\mathbb{P}\big(|W-W_{0}|>\zeta_{1}\big|y_{im},\mathcal{E}_{im},\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}\big)>\zeta_{2}\Big)<\zeta_{2},

where
npLh^{d+4}\leq\log(nh^{d/2-1})
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.3

(Bound for
\big|\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|
)
.

Given the conditions in Section
4.1
, we have that

\sup_{m\in n,\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}},

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Proof.

Consider the following decomposition:

\displaystyle npL\big(\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{i\neq m}(\mathcal{E}_{im}-p)\Bigg\{\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\Big\{\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\psi^{\prime}(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big\}\Bigg\}}_{V_{1}^{m}(\mathbf{x})}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{p\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\Big\{\psi^{\prime}(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\big(\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})-\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})]\big)\Big\}}_{V_{2}^{m}(\mathbf{x})}.

By Assumption
4.5
, there exist constants
c,C
such that

0<c\leq\psi^{\prime}(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\leq C.

Then, we bound
V_{1}^{m}(\mathbf{x})
and
V_{2}^{m}(\mathbf{x})
separately. For
V_{1}^{m}(\mathbf{x})
, for any
m\in[n]
, by Lemma
D.2
, we have

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\big|V_{1}^{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\lesssim npL\sqrt{\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

(C.20)

For
V_{2}^{m}(\mathbf{x})
, we apply the proof scheme of Lemma
D.4
, which yield that for any
m\in[n]
,

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\big|V_{2}^{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\lesssim np^{2}L\sqrt{\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

(C.21)

Combining (
C.20
) and (
C.21
), we have

\sup_{m\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\big|\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{V}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(npLh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}.

∎

Lemma C.4

(Lower Bound of
\mathcal{V}^{*}(\mathbf{x})
)
.

Given the assumptions shown in Section
4.1
, there exists
C\geq 0
such that
\mathcal{V}_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\geq C
for
m\in[n]
.

Proof.

Consider

\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})=\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{1}{npL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\psi^{\prime}(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\Big].

By Assumption
4.5
, there exists some constant
K>0
such that
|\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})|\leq K
. Therefore, there exists
\psi^{\prime}(K)>0
such that
\psi^{\prime}(\theta_{m}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})-\theta_{i}^{*}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}))\geq\psi^{\prime}(K)>0
.
We then have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle\geq\psi^{\prime}(K)\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{1}{npL}\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big]

(C.22)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{npL}\psi^{\prime}(K)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big|\big\{\mathcal{E}_{im}\big\}\Big]\bigg].

By (
C.22
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h^{d}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathcal{K}\Big(\frac{\tau-\mathbf{x}}{h}\Big)f_{\bm{X}}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=\int_{-\mathbf{x}/h}^{(1-\mathbf{x})/h}\mathcal{K}(\sigma)f_{\bm{X}}(\sigma h+\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}\sigma

\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{-\mathbf{x}/h}^{(1-\mathbf{x})/h}\mathcal{K}(\sigma)\mathrm{d}\sigma

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{1/h}\mathcal{K}(\sigma)\mathrm{d}\sigma,

where the third holds as Assumption
4.3
guarantees a lower bound of
f_{\bm{X}}(\cdot)
, and the fourth holds by the symmetricity of
\mathcal{K}(\cdot)
guaranteed by Assumption
4.8
. Therefore, set
h=O(n^{-\epsilon/4})
for some
\epsilon>0
, and we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}^{*}_{m}(\mathbf{x})\geq\frac{1}{npL}\psi^{\prime}(K)\sum_{i\neq m}\sum_{\ell\in[L_{im}]}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathcal{E}_{im}\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big|\big\{\mathcal{E}_{im}\big\}\Big]\bigg]\geq\psi^{\prime}(K)\int_{0}^{1/h}\mathcal{K}(\sigma)d\sigma.

Therefore we have

\mathcal{V}_{m}^{*}(\mathbf{x})\geq C\text{ for any }m\in[n],

which completes the proof
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Technical Lemmas

D.1
Auxiliary Lemmas for Kernel Regression

Note that
Tan et al. (2021)
derive properties of the kernel density estimator for
m
i.i.d. samples
\{X_{1},X_{2},\ldots,X_{m}\}\sim f_{X}(\cdot)
with support on
[0,1]
. A natural generalization for multivariate
\bm{X}\sim f_{\bm{X}}(\cdot)
on a multivariate kernel
\mathcal{K}_{h}
is presented in
Härdle and Müller (1997)
and
Ruppert and Wand (1994)
. Specifically, we define

w_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})=\mathcal{K}_{h}\big(\bm{X}_{i}-\mathbf{x}\big)=K_{h}\big(\|\bm{X}_{i}-\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\big);\quad\mathbb{P}_{m}w_{\mathbf{x}}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{i}-\mathbf{x}).

To facilitate our discussion, we impose the following assumptions: (1) the distribution of
\bm{X}_{i}
satisfies Assumptions
4.2
–
4.3
; (2) the multivariate kernel function
\mathcal{K}(\cdot)
satisfies Assumptions
4.8
–
4.10
. These conditions hold throughout the derivation of the following lemmas.
First, we bound the mean and variance of
\mathbb{P}_{m}w_{\mathbf{x}}
respectively. Lemma
D.1
is a restated version Lemma 2 of
Tan et al. (2021)
, and Lemma
D.2
is a restated version of Lemma 3 of
Tan et al. (2021)
. We provide them here for the self-completeness.

Lemma D.1

(Rates on the Mean of the Kernel Density Estimator)
.

Follow the notations in Section
D.1
, and suppose taht Assumptions
4.2
–
4.3
and
4.8
–
4.10
hold, we have that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{m}w_{\mathbf{x}}]-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\big|=O(h^{2}).

Lemma D.2

(Rate on the Variance of the Kernel Density Estimator)
.

Folow the notations in Section
D.1
, and suppose that Assumptions
4.2
–
4.3
and
4.8
–
4.10
hold, then for a sufficiently large sample size
m
such that
m\gg\log(n/h)/h^{d}
and any real number
\tau>0
, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathbb{P}_{m}w_{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{m}w_{\mathbf{x}}]\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}}\ \ \text{with probability at least}\ \ \ 1-O(n^{-\tau}).

Based on Lemma
D.1
and Lemma
D.2
, we consider a generic framework for a function
g(\cdot):\mathcal{X}=[0,1]^{d}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
. Specifically, suppose that
g(\cdot)
is twice continuously differentiable with uniformly bounded partial derivatives, denoted as
\|g\|_{W^{2,\infty}}\leq C
. Suppose there are
m
i.i.d. samples
\{\bm{X}_{1},\bm{X}_{2},\ldots,\bm{X}_{m}\}\sim f_{\bm{X}}(\cdot)
with support
\Omega\subset\mathcal{X}
. In particular, we consider

v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})=v(\bm{X}_{i},\mathbf{x})=\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{i}-\mathbf{x})g(\bm{X}_{i}),\quad\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{i}-\mathbf{x})g(\bm{X}_{i}),

(D.1)

and

r_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})=r(\bm{X}_{i},\mathbf{x})=\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{i}-\mathbf{x})\epsilon_{i},\quad\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{i}-\mathbf{x})\epsilon_{i}.

(D.2)

Here,
\epsilon_{i}
is independent of
\bm{X}_{i}
and uniformly bounded such that
\sup_{i\in[m]}|\epsilon_{i}|\leq C_{\epsilon}
. We also assume that

\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{i}|\bm{X}_{1:m}]=0
. Lemma
D.3
provides the rate on the bias of the estimator
\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}
by evaluating the difference between its expectation
\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}\big]
and the estimand
f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})g(\mathbf{x})
. Lemma
D.4
evaluates the variance term of
\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}
.

Lemma D.3

(Rates on the Bias of
\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}
)
.

Given Assumptions
4.2
–
4.3
and
4.8
–
4.10
, we have that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}]-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})g(\mathbf{x})\big|=O(h^{2}),\ \ \ \text{ and }\ \ \ \mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}\big]=0.

Proof.

For the bias of
\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}
, we apply Theorem 2.1 of
Ruppert and Wand (1994)
, noting that the regularity conditions for the multivariate kernel
\mathcal{K}
are satisfied under Assumptions
4.8
–
4.10
. Specifically, using the Taylor expansion from Theorem 1 in
Härdle and Müller (1997)
, we obtain

\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}]-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})g(\mathbf{x})=\mu_{2}(\mathcal{K})\big(\nabla g(\mathbf{x})^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}\mathbf{H}\mathbf{H}{{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}}\nabla f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\big)+\frac{1}{2}\mu_{2}(\mathcal{K})f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\mathrm{tr}\Big\{\mathbf{H}{{}^{\scriptscriptstyle\sf T}}\mathcal{H}_{g}(\widetilde{\mathbf{x}})\mathbf{H}\Big\},

where
\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}
lies within a Euclidean ball centered at
\mathbf{x}
with radius
O(h)
. Given that
\mathbf{H}=\mathbf{I}_{d}
for some
d=O(1)
, we conclude that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}]-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})g(\mathbf{x})\big|=O(h^{2}).

For the residual term
\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}
, we apply the law of iterative expectations such that for any
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{1}-\mathbf{x})\epsilon_{1}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{1}-\mathbf{x})\epsilon_{1}\Big|\bm{X}_{1}\Big]\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{1}-\mathbf{x})\mathbb{E}\Big[\epsilon_{1}\Big|\bm{X}_{1}\Big]\Big]=0,

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.4

(Rate on the Variance of
\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}
)
.

Given Assumptions
4.2
–
4.3
and
4.8
–
4.10
, we have that for sample size
m\gg\log(n/h)/h^{d}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}]\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}}\ \ \text{with probability at least}\ 1-O(n^{-\tau});

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}]\big|=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}}\ \ \text{with probability at least}\ 1-O(n^{-\tau}).

Proof.

The proof to Lemma
D.4
is inspired by the proofs of Lemma 4 and Lemma 5 of
Tan et al. (2021)
. Specifically, we apply Lemma
D.18
to control

\mathcal{\mathbb{E}}\bigg\{\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\frac{1}{m}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big|h(\bm{X}_{i};\mathbf{x})-\mathbb{E}\big[h(\bm{X}_{i};\mathbf{x})\big]\Big|\bigg|\bigg\},

with
h(\bm{X}_{i};\mathbf{x})
being either
v_{\mathbf{x}}
or
r_{\mathbf{x}}
. We then apply the concentration inequality in Lemma
D.17
. For the validity of the concentration inequality, weobtain some positive constants
\eta
and
\tau^{2}
such that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|h(\cdot;\mathbf{x})-\mathbb{E}[h(\cdot;\mathbf{x})]\big\|_{\infty}\leq\eta\ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\Big\{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\text{Var}({h(\bm{X}_{i};\mathbf{x})})\Big\}\leq\tau^{2}.

Since
v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)
and
r_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)
are both possible candidates for
h(\cdot;\mathbf{x})
, we consider the two cases separately.

For
v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)
, we consider the function class

\mathcal{V}=\Big\{v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X})-\mathbb{E}[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X})]\big|\mathbf{x}\in\Omega,\bm{X}\sim f_{\bm{X}}(\cdot)\Big\}.

For
\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)-\mathbb{E}[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)]\big\|_{\infty}
, we have that for any
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
,

\displaystyle\big\|v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)-\mathbb{E}[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)]\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)\big\|_{\infty}+\big\|\mathbb{E}[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)]\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\mathcal{K}_{h}(\cdot-\mathbf{x})g(\cdot)\big\|_{\infty}+\big\|\mathbb{E}[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)]\big\|_{\infty}\leq 2C_{g}\Big(\frac{1}{h}\|K\|_{\infty}\Big)^{d}+\big\|\mathbb{E}[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)]\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\frac{2C_{g}\|K\|_{\infty}^{d}}{h^{d}}+\big\|f_{\mathbf{X}}\cdot g\big\|_{\infty}+O(h^{2})\overset{(ii)}{=}C_{1}\frac{\|K\|_{\infty}^{d}}{h^{d}},

for some positive constant
C_{1}
. Here
(i)
holds by Lemma
D.3
for
\mathbb{E}[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)]
and
(ii)
holds by Assumption
4.3
.

For the variance term, we consider the following decomposition:

\begin{split}&\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\text{Var}\big(v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})-\mathbb{E}[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{1})]\big)=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\mathbb{E}\big[(v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})-\mathbb{E}[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{1})])^{2}\big]\\
&\quad\leq 2\mathbb{E}\big[v_{\mathbf{x}}^{2}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]+2\big(\mathbb{E}\big[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\big)^{2}\leq\underbrace{\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}2\mathbb{E}\big[v_{\mathbf{x}}^{2}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}+\underbrace{\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}2\big(\mathbb{E}\big[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\big)^{2}}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}.\end{split}

(D.3)

We then apply the multivariate integration by substitution resembling
(
Ruppert and Wand, 1994
)
. Specifically, for any
\mathbf{x}\in\Omega
,

\mathbb{E}\big[v_{\mathbf{x}}^{2}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\leq\frac{C}{\det(h\mathbf{I}_{d})}\|K\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{C}{h^{d}}\|K\|_{2}^{2}\ \text{ for some positive constant }C.

(D.4)

Furthermore, Lemma
D.3
guarantees that
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
is dominated by
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
. Therefore, combining (
D.3
) and (
D.4
) implies that

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\text{Var}\big(v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})-\mathbb{E}[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{1})]\big)\leq\frac{C_{2}}{h^{d}}\|K\|_{2}^{2}\ \text{ for some positive constant }C_{2}.

Given Assumptions
4.8
-
4.10
for the multivariate kernel function
\mathcal{K}(\cdot)
, Lemma
D.11
implies a covering number bound of the function class
\mathcal{H}=\big\{v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)|\mathbf{x}\in\Omega,\bm{X}\sim f_{\bm{X}}(\cdot)\big\}
. In particular, for some positive constant
C
,

\sup_{\mathcal{Q}}N(\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho)\leq C\Big(\frac{4C_{K}\|K\|_{\infty}\|K\|_{\text{TV}}}{h\rho}\Big)^{4d},

for any probability measure
\mathcal{Q}
and
\rho\in(0,1)
. We then apply Lemma
D.18
with

A=2C^{1/4d}C_{K}\|K\|_{\text{TV}},\ \ \|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{m})}=\Big(\frac{2\|K\|_{\infty}}{h}\Big)^{d},\ \ V=4d,\ \ \sigma_{P}^{2}=\frac{C_{2}\|K\|_{2}^{2}}{h^{d}}.

By
h=o(1)
and
d=O(1)
, we conclude that

\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\frac{1}{m}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big\{v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\big[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\Big\}\bigg|\bigg\}\lesssim\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\log(h^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}}+\frac{\log(h^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}\bigg).

Given the condition on sample size such that
m\gg\log(n/h)/h^{d}
, we have
\frac{\log(h^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}=o(1)
and

\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\frac{1}{m}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big\{v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\big[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\Big\}\bigg|\bigg\}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(h^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}}.

We then apply Lemma
D.17
with

\eta=\frac{C_{1}\|K\|_{\infty}^{d}}{h^{d}},\ \ \tau^{2}=\frac{C_{2}}{h^{d}}\|K\|^{2}_{2},\ \ \mathbb{E}[Y]\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log(h^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}},

which implies that

\mathbb{P}\bigg(\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\frac{1}{m}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big\{v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\big[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\Big\}\bigg|\geq C\bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{\log(h^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}}+\frac{t}{\sqrt{h^{d}}}\sqrt{1+\sqrt{\frac{\log(h^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}}}+\frac{t^{2}}{h^{d}}\bigg\}\bigg)\leq e^{-mt^{2}}.

By taking
t=\sqrt{\frac{\tau\log n}{m}}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{m}v_{\mathbf{x}}]\big|=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\frac{1}{m}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big\{v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\big[v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\Big\}\bigg|

\displaystyle\lesssim\bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{\log(h^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}}+\frac{t}{\sqrt{h^{d}}}\sqrt{1+\sqrt{\frac{\log(h^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}}}+\frac{t^{2}}{h^{d}}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}}

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-\tau})
and completes the proof for
v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)
.

Then, we prove for the residual term
r_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)
.
First, the boundedness of
\epsilon_{i}
implies that for some positive constant
C_{1}^{*}
,

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|r_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)\big\|_{\infty}\leq\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big\|\mathcal{K}_{h}(\cdot-\mathbf{x})\epsilon_{i}\big\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{h^{d}}C_{1}^{*}\|K\|^{d}_{\infty}.

For the variance, we apply Theorem 1 of
Härdle and Müller (1997)
, and we have

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\text{Var}\big(r_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})\big)\leq\frac{C^{*}_{2}}{h^{d}}\|K\|_{2}^{2}\ \text{ for some positive constant }C^{*}_{2}.

Finally, we apply Lemma
D.10
for the covering number of the function class
\mathcal{R}=\big\{r_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X})|\mathbf{x}\in\Omega,\bm{X}\sim f_{\bm{X}}(\cdot)\big\}
. Specifically, for any probability measure
\mathcal{Q}
and
\rho\in(0,1)
, we have

N\{\mathcal{R},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\}\leq C^{*}\Big(\frac{4C_{K}\|K\|_{\infty}\|K\|_{\text{TV}}}{h\rho}\Big)^{4d},

where
C^{*}
is some positive constant. Following a similar argument as for
v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{m}r_{\mathbf{x}}]\big|=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\frac{1}{m}\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{m}\Big\{r_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})-\mathbb{E}\big[r_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X}_{i})\big]\Big\}\bigg|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{mh^{d}}},

with probability at least
1-O(n^{-\tau})
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma
D.5
demonstrates the application of Lemmas
D.1
,
D.2
,
D.3
, and
D.4
to our setting.

In what follows, we denote

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}&=\frac{1}{\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}L_{im}}\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}\Big\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{im}}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\Big\},\\
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}v_{\mathbf{x}}&=\frac{1}{\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}L_{im}}\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}\Big\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{im}}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})g(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell})\Big\},\\
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}&=\frac{1}{\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}L_{im}}\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}\Big\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{L_{im}}\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}_{im}^{\ell}-\mathbf{x})\epsilon_{im}^{\ell}\Big\}.\end{split}

Lemma D.5

(Rates for Erdős–Rényi random graph Random Graph)
.

Given the notations and assumptions shown in Sections
4.1
, we have that

\begin{split}\sup_{m\in[n]}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\Big|\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\Big|&\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\text{ with probability at least }1-O(n^{-10});\\
\sup_{m\in[n]}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\Big|\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}v_{\mathbf{x}}-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})g(\mathbf{x})\Big|&\lesssim h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\text{ with probability at least }1-O(n^{-10});\\
\sup_{m\in[n]}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\big|\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}r_{\mathbf{x}}\big|&\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\text{ with probability at least }1-O(n^{-10}).\end{split}

Proof.

To prove Lemma
D.5
, we first define the event

\mathcal{A}=\bigg\{\sup_{m\in[n]}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\Big|\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\Big|\leq C\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)\text{ for some constant }C\bigg\},

and show that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A})=1-O(n^{-10})
. Specifically, we have that
\mathcal{A}=\cap_{m=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{m}
, where

\mathcal{A}_{m}=\bigg\{\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\Big|\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\Big|\leq C\Big(h^{2}+\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\Big)\text{ for some constant }C\bigg\}.

By the union bound, we have

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A})=1-\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}^{c})=1-\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{m=1}^{n}\mathcal{A}_{m}^{c}\right)\geq 1-\sum_{m=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{c}).

Thus, it suffices to show that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{c})=O(n^{-11})
for any
m\in[n]
.

Applying the law of total probability, we obtain

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{c})=\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{c}\big|\mathcal{B}\big)\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B})+\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{c}\big|\mathcal{B}^{c}\big)\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{c})\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{c}\big|\mathcal{B}\big)+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{c})=1-\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}\big|\mathcal{B}\big)+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{c}),

where the event
\mathcal{B}
is defined as

\mathcal{B}=\Big\{\frac{np}{2}\leq\min_{i\in[n]}{d_{i}}\leq\max_{i\in[n]}{d_{i}}\leq\frac{3np}{2}\Big\}.

Lemma
D.13
ensures that
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{c})=O(n^{-11})
in the context of the Erdős–Rényi random graph
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{E})
. To bound
\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}\mid\mathcal{B}\big)
, we decompose the supremum into two parts:

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\left|\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\right|\leq\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\left|\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}]\right|+\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}]-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\right|.

Define the events

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{m}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\left\{\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\left|\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}-\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}]\right|\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log(nh^{d/2-1})}{npLh^{d}}}\text{ for some constant }C\right\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{m}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=\left\{\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\Omega}\left|\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}]-f_{\bm{X}}(\mathbf{x})\right|\leq Ch^{2}\text{ for some constant }C\right\}.

Since
\mathcal{A}_{m}^{(1)}\cap\mathcal{A}_{m}^{(2)}
implies
\mathcal{A}_{m}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}\mid\mathcal{B}\big)\geq\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{(1)}\cap\mathcal{A}_{m}^{(2)}\mid\mathcal{B}\big),

leading to

1-\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}\mid\mathcal{B}\big)\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{(1)c}\cup\mathcal{A}_{m}^{(2)c}\mid\mathcal{B}\big)\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{(1)c}\mid\mathcal{B}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{(2)c}\mid\mathcal{B}\big)=O(n^{-11}).

Here, the last equality follows by applying Lemmas
D.1
and
D.2
to the samples
\sum_{i\neq m}\mathcal{E}_{im}L_{im}
. Thus, for any
m\in[n]
,

\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}^{c}\big)\leq\Big(1-\mathbb{P}\big(\mathcal{A}_{m}\big|\mathcal{B}\big)\Big)+\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{B}^{c})=O(n^{-11}),

completing the proof for
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}w_{\mathbf{x}}
. The proofs for
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}v_{\mathbf{x}}
and
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{E}}r_{\mathbf{x}}
follow similarly by substituting Lemmas
D.1
and
D.2
with Lemmas
D.3
and
D.4
, respectively.
∎

D.2
Auxiliary Lemmas for Covering Number

We present auxiliary lemmas concerning the covering number for specific classes of functions. These lemmas are crucial for satisfying the covering number conditions of the function family
\mathcal{F}
in Lemma
D.18
and for controlling the variance term in kernel regression in Lemma
D.2
.

Lemma
D.6
is a restated version of Lemma 3 in
Giné and Nickl (2009)
, and we provide it here for completeness.

Lemma D.6

(Bound on Covering Number for Functions of Bounded Variation)
.

Let
K(\cdot):\mathbb{R}\xrightarrow{}\mathbb{R}
be a function of bounded variation. Define the function class
\mathcal{F}_{h}=\big\{K\big(\frac{x-\cdot}{h}\big)\big|x\in\mathbb{R}\big\}
. Then there exists
C_{K}<\infty
independent of
h
and
K
such that for any
0<\rho<1
and probability measure
\mathcal{Q}
, the covering number for
\mathcal{F}_{h}
with
L^{2}(\mathcal{Q})
-norm distance and radius
\rho
satisfies

\sup_{\mathcal{Q}}\{N\{\mathcal{F}_{h},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\}\}\leq\bigg(\frac{2C_{K}\|K\|_{\text{TV}}}{\rho}\bigg)^{4},

where
\|K\|_{\text{TV}}
is the total variation norm of the function
K
.

Furthermore, Lemma
D.7
is a restated version of Lemma 14 of
Tan et al. (2021)
. It provides a bound of the covering number for a set of Lipschitz functions defined on
[0,1]
.

Lemma D.7

(Bound on Covering Number for Lipschitz Function)
.

Let
f(\ell)
be a Lipschitz function defined on
[0,1]
such that
|f(\ell)-f(\ell^{\prime})|\leq L_{f}|\ell-\ell^{\prime}|
for any
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in[0,1]
.
L_{f}
is the Lipschitz coefficient. The function class is defined as
\mathcal{F}=\{g_{\ell}=f(\ell)|\ell\in[0,1]\}
. For any
\rho\in(0,1)
and probability measure
\mathcal{Q}
, the covering number of the funcion class
\mathcal{F}
satisties that

N\{\mathcal{F},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\}\leq\frac{L_{f}}{\rho}.

Finally, Lemma
D.8
is a restated version of Lemma 15 of
Tan et al. (2021)
. It provides a bound on the covering number for combined functional classes under addition and multiplication.

Lemma D.8

(Bound on Covering Number for Combined Functional Classes)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
be two function classes satisfying

N\{\mathcal{F}_{1},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),a_{1}\rho\}\leq C_{1}\rho^{-v_{1}}\ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ N\{\mathcal{F}_{2},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),a_{2}\rho\}\leq C_{2}\rho^{-v_{2}}

for some
C_{1},C_{2},a_{1},a_{2},v_{1},v_{2}>0
and any
\rho\in(0,1)
. Define that
\|\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell}}\|f\|_{\infty}
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, and
U=\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\{\|\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\}
. Consider the multiplicative class
\mathcal{F}_{\times}=\{f_{1}f_{2}|f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{2}\}
and the additive class
\mathcal{F}_{+}=\{f_{1}+f_{2}|f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{2}\}
, we have that for any
\rho\in(0,1)
,

N\{\mathcal{F}_{\times},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\}\leq C_{1}C_{2}\Big(\frac{2a_{1}U}{\rho}\Big)^{v_{1}}\Big(\frac{2a_{2}U}{\rho}\Big)^{v_{2}};\ \ N\{\mathcal{F}_{+},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\}\leq C_{1}C_{2}\Big(\frac{2a_{1}}{\rho}\Big)^{v_{1}}\Big(\frac{2a_{2}}{\rho}\Big)^{v_{2}}.

Building on the proving technique used in Lemma
D.8
, we establish a bound for the covering number of
multiplicative
kernels, as stated in Lemma
D.9
.

Lemma D.9

(Bound on Covering Number for Multiplicative Kernels)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
be function classes defined on the domains
\mathcal{X}_{1}
and
\mathcal{X}_{2}
, respectively. Assume that

N\{\mathcal{F}_{1},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),a_{1}\rho\}\leq C_{1}\rho^{-v_{1}}\ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ N\{\mathcal{F}_{2},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),a_{2}\rho\}\leq C_{2}\rho^{-v_{2}}

for some constants
C_{1},C_{2},a_{1},a_{2},v_{1},v_{2}>0
, and for any probability measure
\mathcal{Q}
and
\rho\in(0,1)
. Define
\|\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\ell}}\|f\|_{\infty}
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
, and let
U=\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\{\|\mathcal{F}_{\ell}\|_{\infty}\}
. Consider the
multiplicative
function class
\mathcal{F}_{\times}
defined as

\mathcal{F}_{\times}=\Big\{f(x_{1},x_{2})=f_{1}(x_{1})f_{2}(x_{2})\big|f_{1}\in\mathcal{F}_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}_{2}\Big\}.

Then, for any
\rho\in(0,1)
and any probability measure
\mathcal{Q}
on
\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2}
, the covering number satisfies

N\big\{\mathcal{F}_{\times},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\big\}\leq C_{1}C_{2}\Big(\frac{2a_{1}U}{\rho}\Big)^{v_{1}}\Big(\frac{2a_{2}U}{\rho}\Big)^{v_{2}}.

Proof.

To prove Lemma
D.9
, we construct a
\rho
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\times}
for the function class
\mathcal{F}_{\times}
. For any
\rho\in(0,1)
, let
\mathcal{N}_{1}=\{f_{11},f_{12},\dots,f_{1N_{1}}\}
and
\mathcal{N}_{2}=\{f_{21},f_{22},\dots,f_{2N_{2}}\}
be the
\rho/(2U)
-nets of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}
with respect to the probability measures
\mathcal{Q}_{1}
and
\mathcal{Q}_{2}
, respectively. For any functions
h_{1}(\cdot)
and
h_{2}(\cdot)
, we have

\int_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2}}h_{1}(x_{1})\,d\mathcal{Q}(x_{1},x_{2})=\int_{\mathcal{X}_{1}}h_{1}(x_{1})\,d\mathcal{Q}_{1}(x_{1}),

and

\int_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2}}h_{2}(x_{2})\,d\mathcal{Q}(x_{1},x_{2})=\int_{\mathcal{X}_{2}}h_{2}(x_{2})\,d\mathcal{Q}_{2}(x_{2}).

The covering numbers
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
are therefore bounded that

N_{1}=N\big\{\mathcal{N}_{1},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}_{1}),\rho/2U\big\}\leq C_{1}\Big(\frac{2a_{1}U}{\rho}\Big)^{v_{1}},\ N_{2}=N\big\{\mathcal{N}_{2},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}_{2}),\rho/2U\big\}\leq C_{2}\Big(\frac{2a_{2}U}{\rho}\Big)^{v_{2}}.

(D.5)

Now, define the
\rho
-net
\mathcal{N}_{\times}
for
\mathcal{F}_{\times}
as:

\mathcal{N}_{\times}=\Big\{f(x_{1},x_{2})=f_{1}^{(j)}(x_{1})f_{2}^{(k)}(x_{2})\big|f_{1}^{(j)}\in\mathcal{N}_{1},f_{2}^{(k)}\in\mathcal{N}_{2}\Big\}.

We next show that
\mathcal{N}_{\times}
is indeed a
\rho
-net for
\mathcal{F}_{\times}
. Specifically, for any
f=f_{1}(x_{1})f_{2}(x_{2})\in\mathcal{F}_{\times}
, there exist functions
f_{1j}\in\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
f_{2k}\in\mathcal{N}_{2}
such that
\widetilde{f}=f_{1j}(x_{1})f_{2k}(x_{2})\in\mathcal{N}_{\times}
satisfies
\|f_{1}-f_{1j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})}\leq\rho/(2U)
and
\|f_{2}-f_{2k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}_{2})}\leq\rho/(2U)
.
Consider the
L^{2}(\mathcal{Q})
-norm of the difference
f-\widetilde{f}
that

\big\|f-\widetilde{f}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Q})}^{2}=\int_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2}}\Big|f_{1}(x_{1})f_{2}(x_{2})-f_{1j}(x_{1})f_{2k}(x_{2})\Big|^{2}d\mathcal{Q}(x_{1},x_{2}).

By (
D.5
), we apply the triangle inequality that

\displaystyle\|f-\widetilde{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Q})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2}}\left[f_{1}(x_{1})^{2}\left(f_{2}(x_{2})-f_{2k}(x_{2})\right)^{2}+f_{2k}(x_{2})^{2}\left(f_{1}(x_{1})-f_{1j}(x_{1})\right)^{2}\right]d\mathcal{Q}(x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle\leq 2U^{2}\left(\|f_{1}-f_{1j}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}_{1})}^{2}+\|f_{2}-f_{2k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}_{2})}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2U^{2}\left[\left(\frac{\rho}{2U}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{\rho}{2U}\right)^{2}\right]=\rho^{2}.

Therefore,
\mathcal{N}_{\times}
is a
\rho
-net for
\mathcal{F}_{\times}
, and the covering number is bounded by

N\big\{\mathcal{F}_{\times},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\big\}\leq N_{1}N_{2}=C_{1}C_{2}\Big(\frac{2a_{1}U}{\rho}\Big)^{v_{1}}\Big(\frac{2a_{2}U}{\rho}\Big)^{v_{2}}.

for any probability measure
\mathcal{Q}
and
\rho\in(0,1)
, which completes the proof.
∎

To prove Lemma
D.2
, we apply Lemma
D.9
for the covering number of the multivariate kernel and generalize Lemma 3 from
Tan et al. (2021)
. Similarly, we need to verify the covering number condition for the function class
\mathcal{R}=\left\{r_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X})\mid\mathbf{x}\in\Omega,\bm{X}\sim f_{\bm{X}}(\cdot)\right\}
.

Lemma D.10

(Covering Number Condition for
\mathcal{R}
)
.

Suppose the density function
f_{\bm{X}}(\cdot)
satisfies Assumptions
4.2
–
4.3
, and the kernel function
\mathcal{K}(\cdot)
satisfies Assumptions
4.8
–
4.10
. Then, for any probability measure
\mathcal{Q}
and
\rho\in(0,1)
,

N\{\mathcal{R},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\}\leq C_{\epsilon}^{4}\Big(\frac{4C_{K}\|K\|_{\infty}\|K\|_{\text{TV}}}{h\rho}\Big)^{4d}.

Proof.

Since the kernel
\mathcal{K}_{h}
is
multiplicative
, for
\bm{X}=\{X_{1},X_{2},\dots,X_{d}\}
and
\mathbf{x}=\{x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{d}\}
, we have

\big|r_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X})\big|=\big|\mathcal{K}_{h}(\bm{X}-\mathbf{x})\epsilon\big|=\bigg|\Big(\prod_{\ell=1}^{d}K_{h}(X_{\ell}-x_{\ell})\Big)\epsilon\bigg|\leq C_{\epsilon}\prod_{\ell=1}^{d}K_{h}(X_{\ell}-x_{\ell}).

Applying Lemma
D.6
to each
K_{h}(X_{\ell}-x_{\ell})
and Lemma
D.9
with
U=\|K\|_{\infty}
, we obtain that for any probability measure
\mathcal{Q}
and
\rho\in(0,1)
,

N\{\mathcal{R},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\}\leq C_{\epsilon}^{4}\bigg(\Big(\frac{2\|K\|_{\infty}(2C_{K}\|K\|_{\text{TV}})}{h\rho}\Big)^{4}\bigg)^{d}=C_{\epsilon}^{4}\Big(\frac{4C_{K}\|K\|_{\infty}\|K\|_{\text{TV}}}{h\rho}\Big)^{4d}.

∎

Given that
h=o(1)
, we establish the covering number condition for the function class
\mathcal{H}=\left\{v_{\mathbf{x}}(\cdot)\mid\mathbf{x}\in\Omega,\bm{X}\sim f_{\bm{X}}(\cdot)\right\}
, as stated in Lemma
D.11
. We assume that the regularity conditions on the density function
f_{\bm{X}}(\cdot)
and the kernel function
K(\cdot)
hold as described in Section
4.1
.

Lemma D.11

(Covering Number Condition for
\mathcal{H}
)
.

Suppose that the density function
f_{\bm{X}}(\cdot)
satisfies Assumptions
4.2
–
4.3
and the kernel function
\mathcal{K}(\cdot)
satisfies Assumptions
4.8
–
4.10
. Then, for any probability measure
\mathcal{Q}
and
\rho\in(0,1)
, we have

\sup_{\mathcal{Q}}N\{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\}\leq 16C_{g}^{4}\Big(\frac{4C_{K}\|K\|_{\infty}\|K\|_{\text{TV}}}{h\rho}\Big)^{4d}.

Proof.

To evaluate
\sup_{\mathcal{Q}}N\left\{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\right\}
, we first consider the boundedness of
g(\cdot)
, such that
\|g\|_{\infty}\leq C_{g}
. Consequently, we have

\big|v_{\mathbf{x}}(\bm{X})\big|=\bigg|\Big(\prod_{\ell=1}^{d}K_{h}(X_{\ell}-x_{\ell})\Big)\big(g(\bm{X})-g(\mathbf{x})\big)\bigg|\leq 2C_{g}\prod_{\ell=1}^{d}K_{h}(X_{\ell}-x_{\ell}).

We then apply Lemma
D.6
to each
K_{h}(X_{\ell}-x_{\ell})
and Lemma
D.9
with
U=\|K\|_{\infty}
, which yields that for any probability measure
\mathcal{Q}
and
\rho\in(0,1)
,

N\{\mathcal{H},L^{2}(\mathcal{Q}),\rho\}\leq(2C_{g})^{4}\bigg(\Big(\frac{2\|K\|_{\infty}(2C_{K}\|K\|_{\text{TV}})}{h\rho}\Big)^{4}\bigg)^{d}=16C_{g}^{4}\Big(\frac{4C_{K}\|K\|_{\infty}\|K\|_{\text{TV}}}{h\rho}\Big)^{4d},

completing the proof.
∎

In the proof of Theorem
4.5
, we apply Lemma 5.7 from
Van Handel (2014)
, which requires calculating the covering numbers of
(t,t^{\prime})\in\bm{X}^{2}_{\epsilon}
, where
\bm{X}^{2}_{\epsilon}
is defined in (
A.12
). We present Lemma
D.12
, which provides the necessary bounds under Assumption
4.13
.

Lemma D.12

(Covering Number for
\bm{X}^{2}_{\epsilon}
)
.

The covering number
N(\bm{X}^{2}_{\epsilon},d_{\|\cdot\|}(\cdot,\cdot),\widetilde{\epsilon})
is bounded by

N(\bm{X}^{2}_{\epsilon},d_{\|\cdot\|}(\cdot,\cdot),\widetilde{\epsilon})\leq N(\bm{X},\|\cdot\|,\widetilde{\epsilon}/2)\cdot N(B_{\|\cdot\|}(t,\epsilon+\widetilde{\epsilon}),\|\cdot\|,\widetilde{\epsilon}/2),

(D.6)

where
\bm{X}^{2}_{\epsilon}
is defined in (
A.12
) and the distance metric
d_{\|\cdot\|}
is given by

d_{\|\cdot\|}((t_{1},t_{2}),(t^{\prime}_{1},t^{\prime}_{2}))=\|t_{1}-t^{\prime}_{1}\|+\|t_{2}-t^{\prime}_{2}\|.

Proof.

We begin by constructing a
\widetilde{\epsilon}/2
-net for
\bm{X}
, denoted as
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}/2}
. According to the definition of an
\epsilon
-net, for every
\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}
, there exists a point
\pi(t)\in\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}/2}
such that

\|t-\pi(t)\|\leq\widetilde{\epsilon}/2.

Next, we consider the
\|\cdot\|
-ball
B_{\|\cdot\|}(t,\epsilon+\widetilde{\epsilon})=\{\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\bm{X}\mid\|t^{\prime}-t\|\leq\epsilon+\widetilde{\epsilon}\}
. We construct a
\widetilde{\epsilon}/2
-cover for this ball, denoted as
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}/2,t}
. By the definition of this cover, for every
s\in B_{\|\cdot\|}(t,\epsilon+\widetilde{\epsilon})
, there exists a point
q_{t}(s)\in\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}/2,t}
such that

\|s-q_{t}(s)\|\leq\widetilde{\epsilon}/2.

Considering the arbitrary choice of
t
and
s
, we construct the
\widetilde{\epsilon}
-net for
\bm{X}_{\epsilon}^{2}
as

\mathcal{N}^{*}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}}=\{(t_{0},t^{\prime}_{0})\mid t_{0}\in\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}/2},t^{\prime}_{0}\in\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}/2,t_{0}}\}.

For any pair
(t,t^{\prime})\in\bm{X}_{\epsilon}^{2}
, the
\epsilon
-net definition guarantees the existence of
\pi(t)\in\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}/2}
and
q_{\pi(t)}(t^{\prime})\in\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}/2,\pi(t)}
. By applying the triangle inequality, we have

\|t^{\prime}-\pi(t)\|\leq\|t^{\prime}-t\|+\|t-\pi(t)\|.

It follows that
t^{\prime}
lies within the
\widetilde{\epsilon}/2
-cover
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}/2,\pi(t)}
, ensuring that
q_{\pi(t)}(t^{\prime})\in\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}/2,\pi(t)}
. Consequently, we can identify a pair
(\pi(t),q_{\pi(t)}(t^{\prime}))\in\mathcal{N}^{*}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}}
such that:

d_{\|\cdot\|}((t,t^{\prime}),(\pi(t),q_{\pi(t)}(t^{\prime})))=\|t-\pi(t)\|+\|t^{\prime}-q_{\pi(t)}(t^{\prime})\|\leq\frac{\widetilde{\epsilon}}{2}+\frac{\widetilde{\epsilon}}{2}=\widetilde{\epsilon}.

This construction demonstrates that
\mathcal{N}^{*}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}}
is a
\widetilde{\epsilon}
-net for
\bm{X}_{\epsilon}^{2}
, consistent with the definition of an
\epsilon
-net. The covering number of
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}/2}
is bounded as shown in (
D.6
), which is consistent with the setup of
\mathcal{N}^{*}_{\widetilde{\epsilon}}
.
∎

D.3
Auxiliary Lemmas for Concentration Property

We present key results in the form of concentration inequalities that underpin the derived rates. Lemma
D.13
and Lemma
D.14
establish bounds for vertex degrees in an Erdős–Rényi random graph, leveraging fundamental concentration results such as the Chernoff bound. Lemma
D.16
introduces a variant of the Bernstein inequality. Lemma
D.17
shows that the supremum of certain empirical processes, under mild regularization conditions, converges to its mean. The validity of Lemma
D.17
is supported by Lemma
D.18
, which relies on covering entropy conditions discussed in Section
D.2
.

Lemma
D.13
is a restated version of Lemma 1 in
Chen et al. (2019)
. It follows directly from the Chernoff bound, with an additional application of the union bound over
i\in[n]
.

Lemma D.13

(Bound on the Degree of Erdős–Rényi Random Graph)
.

Consider
\mathcal{E}_{ij}
as the indicator of whether the proposed edge
(i,j)
exists in the Erdős–Rényi random graph
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{V},\mathcal{E})
. The degree of vertex
i
is denoted as
d_{i}
where
d_{i}=\sum_{j\neq i;j\in[n]}\mathcal{E}_{ij}
. If
p
satisfies Assumption
4.1
, then the event

\mathcal{A}_{1}=\bigg\{\frac{np}{2}\leq\min_{i\in[n]}d_{i}\leq\max_{i\in[n]}d_{i}\leq\frac{3np}{2}\bigg\}

(D.7)

occurs with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Lemma
D.14
is a direct corollary of Lemma
D.13
.

Lemma D.14

(Bound on the Total Number of Edges of Erdős–Rényi Random Graph)
.

The event

\mathcal{A}_{2}=\bigg\{\frac{n^{2}p}{4}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\leq 2n^{2}p\bigg\}

occurs with probability at least
1-O(n^{-10})
.

Proof.

We show that
\mathcal{A}_{1}\subset\mathcal{A}_{2}
. Assuming that
\mathcal{A}_{1}
holds, it follows that
\mathcal{A}_{2}
also holds because

\frac{n^{2}p}{4}<\frac{n^{2}p}{2}\leq n\Big\{\min_{i\in[n]}d_{i}\Big\}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{i}\leq n\Big\{\max_{i\in[n]}d_{i}\Big\}\leq\frac{3n^{2}p}{2}<2n^{2}p.

Therefore,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{1})\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{A}_{2})
, and Lemma
D.14
follows directly from Lemma
D.13
.

∎

Remark D.15
.

For Lemma
D.13
and Lemma
D.14
, the specific constant in the tail probability
O(n^{-10})
is not crucial. Both lemmas remain valid with a tail probability of
O(n^{-\tau})
for any positive constant
\tau
, provided that the constant
C_{0}=C_{0}(\tau)
is sufficiently large.

Lemma
D.16
is a version of the Bernstein inequality as presented by
Boucheron et al. (2013)
. For brevity, the proof is omitted.

Lemma D.16

(Bernstein Inequality)
.

Consider
n
independent random variables
Z_{\ell}
,
\ell\in[n]
, each satisfying
|Z_{\ell}|\leq B
. For any
a\geq 2
, the following holds with probability at least
1-2n^{-a}
:

\left|\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}Z_{\ell}-\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}Z_{\ell}\right]\right|\leq\sqrt{2a\log n\sum_{\ell=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell}^{2}]}+\frac{2a}{3}B\log n.

Lemmas
D.17
and
D.18
introduce a series of inequalities that bound the expectation of the supremum of an empirical process. A typical approach involves first applying Lemma
D.18
to bound
\mathbb{E}[Y]
as defined in Lemma
D.17
. Then, Lemma
D.17
establishes the concentration for the supremum of the empirical process. This technique is discussed in
Bousquet (2002)
,
Van de Geer (2008)
,
Koltchinskii (2011)
, and
Lu et al. (2018)
.

Lemma D.17

(Concentration on the Supreme of Empirical Process)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
be independent random variables and let
\mathcal{F}
be a function class such that there exists
\eta
and
\tau^{2}
satisfying

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\|f\|_{\infty}\leq\eta\ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in n}\mathrm{Var}\{f(X_{i})\}\leq\tau^{2}.

Define

Y=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}[f(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}\{f(X_{i})\}]\bigg|.

Then for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\Big[Y\geq\mathbb{E}[Y]+t\sqrt{2\{\tau^{2}+2\eta\mathbb{E}[Y]\}}+2t^{2}\eta/3\Big]\leq\exp{(-nt^{2})}.

Lemma D.18

(Upper Bound on the Expectation of the Supreme of the Empirical Process)
.

Assume that the functions in
\mathcal{F}
, defined on
\mathcal{X}
, are uniformly bounded by a constant
U
. Let
F(\cdot)
be the envelope function of
\mathcal{F}
, such that
|f(x)|\leq F(x)
for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
and
f\in\mathcal{F}
. Define
\sigma_{P}^{2}=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\mathbb{E}(f^{2})
. Let
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
be i.i.d. copies of the random variable
X
, and denote the empirical measure by
\mathbb{P}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\delta_{X_{i}}
.

If for some constants
A,V>0
, and for all
\epsilon>0
and
n\geq 1
, the covering entropy satisfies

N\{\mathcal{F},L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n}),\epsilon\}\leq\bigg(\frac{A\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n})}}{\epsilon}\bigg)^{V},

then for any i.i.d. sub-Gaussian mean-zero random variables
\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n}
, there exists a universal constant
C
such that

\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{n}\bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}\xi_{i}f(X_{i})\bigg|\bigg\}\leq C\bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{V}{n}}\sigma_{P}\sqrt{\log\bigg(\frac{A\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P})}}{\sigma_{P}}\bigg)}+\frac{VU}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{A\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P})}}{\sigma_{P}}\bigg)\bigg\}.

Furthermore, we have

\mathbb{E}\bigg\{\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\frac{1}{n}\bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}[f(X_{i})-\mathbb{E}[f(X_{i})]\bigg|\bigg\}\leq C\bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{V}{n}}\sigma_{P}\sqrt{\log\bigg(\frac{A\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P})}}{\sigma_{P}}\bigg)}+\frac{VU}{n}\log\bigg(\frac{A\|F\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{P})}}{\sigma_{P}}\bigg)\bigg\}.

In Section
4
, we require a new version of Bousquet’s inequality, as presented in Lemma
D.19
. This version applies to independent functions
f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})
that have uniformly bounded infinite norms and variances, though they may not be identical. Inspired by
Bousquet (2002)
, this version captures the concentration for the maximum of the empirical process
\sum_{i\in[n]}f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})
to
\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}
.

Lemma D.19

(Concentration on Independent Empirical Process)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
be independent random variables. Assume that there exist uniform constants
\eta
and
\tau^{2}
satisfying

\sup_{i\in[n],\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\|f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})\|_{\infty}\leq\eta\ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathrm{Var}\{f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})\}\leq\tau^{2}.

Define

Y=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}[f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})-\mathbb{E}\{f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})\}]\bigg|.

Then for any
u>0
,

\mathbb{P}\Big[Y\geq\mathbb{E}[Y]+u\sqrt{2\{\tau^{2}+2\eta\mathbb{E}[Y]\}}+2u^{2}\eta/3\Big]\leq\exp{(-nu^{2})}.

(D.8)

Proof.

To prove this lemma, we verify the conditions of Theorem 2.1 from
Bousquet (2002)
. Without loss of generality, we consider the case where
\mathbb{E}[f_{i}]=0
for any
i\in[n]
. To align our notation with
Bousquet (2002)
, we introduce the leave-one-out supremum for each
k\in[n]
with the corresponding parameter
\mathbf{x}_{k}
defined as

Z_{k}=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n];i\neq k}f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})\Bigg|,\ \ \ \mathbf{x}_{k}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n];i\neq k}f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})\Bigg|.

For the overall supremum, we define

Z=\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})\Bigg|,\ \ \ \mathbf{x}^{*}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})\Bigg|.

The proof requires us to verify conditions (2) and (3) of Theorem 2.1 in
Bousquet (2002)
. For condition (2), under the construction described above, we have that

Z_{k}^{\prime}=\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{k},X_{i})\Bigg|-Z_{k}=\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{k},X_{i})\Bigg|-\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n];i\neq k}f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})\Bigg|.

(D.9)

From the definition of the supremum, we have

\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{k},X_{i})\Bigg|\leq\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{*},X_{i})\Bigg|\ \ \ \text{and}\ \ \ \Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n];i\neq k}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{*},X_{i})\Bigg|\leq\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n];i\neq k}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{k},X_{i})\Bigg|.

(D.10)

Applying (
D.9
) and (
D.10
) yields that

Z_{k}^{\prime}\leq\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{*},X_{i})\Bigg|-\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n];i\neq k}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{*},X_{i})\Bigg|\leq|f_{k}(\mathbf{x}^{*},X_{k})|\leq\eta\ \ \text{almost surely}.

We then apply the conditional expectation
\mathbb{E}^{k}_{n}[\cdot]
, defined as
\mathbb{E}[\cdot\mid X_{i};i\in[n],i\neq k]
, to
Z_{k}^{\prime}
, and we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{k}_{n}[Z_{k}^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{n}^{k}\Bigg[\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{k},X_{i})\Bigg|\Bigg]-\mathbb{E}^{k}_{n}[Z_{k}]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\mathbb{E}_{n}^{k}\Bigg[\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{k},X_{i})\Bigg|\Bigg]-Z_{k}\overset{(ii)}{\geq}\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{n}^{k}\Bigg[\sum_{i\in[n]}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{k},X_{i})\Bigg]\Bigg|-Z_{k}\overset{(iii)}{=}0.

Here
(i)
holds since
Z_{k}
has no randomness with respect to
X_{k}
;
(ii)
holds through Jensen’s inequality where the function
\phi(x)=|x|
is convex, and
(iii)
holds by
\mathbb{E}[f_{k}]=0
for any
k\in[n]
.

For condition (3), we conclude that

\displaystyle(n-1)Z

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n]}(n-1)f_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{*},X_{i})\Bigg|

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\Bigg|\sum_{k\in[n]}\sum_{i\in[n],i\neq k}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{*},X_{i})\Bigg|\overset{(ii)}{\leq}\sum_{k\in[n]}\Bigg|\sum_{i\in[n],i\neq k}f_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{*},X_{i})\Bigg|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}Z_{k}.

Here, (i) follows from the leave-one-out structure; (ii) holds by the triangle inequality, and (iii) holds by the definition of
Z_{k}
as a supremum.

Finally, applying the triangle inequality to
Z_{k}^{\prime}
, we get
|Z_{k}^{\prime}|\leq|f_{k}(\mathbf{x}_{k},X_{k})|
. Thus, a bound
\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\bm{X}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathrm{Var}\{f_{i}(\mathbf{x},X_{i})\}\leq\tau^{2}
guarantees the desired rate.
∎
